## Outcome

628 inventory ids (DETERMINISM-0001 … DETERMINISM-0628): 570 normative items plus 58 defined terms. By kind: rule 219, term 58, edit-kind 56, invariant 50, table-row 40, mechanism 36, lifecycle-row 35, number 25, failure-class 24, interface 21, test 19, type 16, table 14, bar 7, step 5, provenance 1, list 1, defect 1.

Ownership (the `owner` field): determinism 583, volume 12, pipeline 11, model 8, rendering 7, kernel 6, scope 1. Volume subjects the unit carries (hooks, staged rows, records the volumes build on): V03 interiors 18, V04 underground 16, V08 SettlementForge 11, V06 evolution 10, V02 buildings 5, V05 sky 4, V09 application 4, V01 neighbourhoods 3, V10 clerk 3. The unit cites no volume by id; every volume subject is reached through PART II, II.6-II.7, "staged" rows or amendment items, and 83 unapplied f items route the volumes' hooks into it (routing below).

Findings: 6 cross-part conflicts (2 major), 12 internal conflicts or ambiguities, 6 wiring gaps (1 major), 2 references that do not resolve cleanly (0 unresolved at section level; 70 qualified references checked), 22 cross-part restatements, 20 duplications inside the unit, 10 elegance candidates, 9 edges observed without a stated outcome.

Wiring convention: `reads` = what the item depends on or is produced by; `used by` = what reads, calls, renders, exports or tests it. In-unit ends are inventory ids; outside ends are section and name. `outside_mentions` (JSON only) lists the base sections outside §5-§7 and the volumes whose text names the item's identifier-like keys (script), for the wiring graph: 438 items have keys, 407 are named in at least one volume.

## Findings for the canon

Evidence: CONFIRMED by grep or script: DXC-04 (V09 §4.3 table read), DXC-05 (V11 §2.1 heading; grep for reroll.maxAdvance and crossing.searchBandM), DIC-01, DIC-06 (T8-n and T12-1 in §13.2), DWG-02 (Diff defined nowhere), DWG-03, DWG-06 (f and g files parsed), DBR-01 (both §4.5 lists read). The other DIC and DWG items are readings of the unit (PLAUSIBLE until the canon rules); DWG-05 is PLAUSIBLE (the registry's per-kind anchor paths were not enumerated). Nothing here is a ruling.

### Wiring gaps

| id | sev | kind | items | claim | missing link |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DWG-01 | major | undefined outcome / dangling output | DETERMINISM-0027, DETERMINISM-0043, DETERMINISM-0109, DETERMINISM-0111 | Three ladders end in "a bug that aborts the batch (or rung) with its stream key": F-NUM on a mandatory chain's E_OUT_OF_RANGE, F-PLAN's refusal of a seed, passage or composer ring, and F-PLAN's tier 6 when the capacity-bounded household count cannot be cut. §5.3 defines a hard invariant's abort (the batch rolls back, its ladder continues), but a bug abort has no stated outcome — whether the stage continues, what the building or chain becomes, what the user sees — and the stream key it "reports" has no field: GenerationReport records stream keys only for ladder uses. | §5.4 gains a bug/abort list (class, entity, stream key, stage) and §5.1 states the outcome of a bug abort (e.g. the batch rolls back and its entity takes the class's last rung, reported), with a test in §13.1. |
| DWG-02 | minor | undefined type | DETERMINISM-0111 | Type Diff (GenerationReport.requestedVsBuilt: Diff[]; §14's migrate returns { doc, diff: Diff }) is defined nowhere in the base or the twelve volumes (grep). | Define Diff once (its fields: requested value, built value, reason) at §5.4, and name §7.5's diff shape (entities changed, orphans, changed confirmations, statistics deltas) as the same or a separate type. |
| DWG-03 | minor | table value without a table | DETERMINISM-0019, DETERMINISM-0017, DETERMINISM-0190, DETERMINISM-0417, DETERMINISM-0194 | reroll.maxAdvance ("a table value, default 3"; read by §5.1 (6), §6.3 case (3) and §7.3 item 10) and crossing.searchBandM ("default 120 m"; §4.2 S1 (5), §6.3's bridge anchor radius) are called table values, but no table in §7.6's list, §14's @plat/tables or V11's registry carries them. | Name each value's table and row (and its range for the tables editor), or declare it a constant. |
| DWG-04 | minor | field without semantics | DETERMINISM-0111 | GenerationReport.stages[].attempts: number has no producer rule and no reader; nothing says what it counts (tier-3 retries, all ladder uses, kernel attempts). | State what it counts and who reads it (the report panel, §13.2's cost tests), or drop it (ladderUses already records every retry). |
| DWG-05 | minor | registry path without its row (PLAUSIBLE) | DETERMINISM-0194, DETERMINISM-0184 | §2.8's entity registry declares an anchor encoding, hysteresis radius and class family per type "(§6.3)", and §6.3 says every re-derived entity is matched by its anchor; §6.3's anchor table has no row for several re-derivable kinds the unit names — yard parts (S11 units, §6.3's derivation units), a block's Frontage record (only its split rule is stated), Corridor records, the Castle, decorations and institutions — so their succession on re-derivation is undefined. | Add their anchor rows (or state that they are re-derived without succession, their references carried by a named rule). |
| DWG-06 | minor | routing (F-MISSING at assembly) | DETERMINISM-0349, DETERMINISM-0563 | g items reaching the program after the volumes pass name this unit as a home and have no text in it yet: item 111 (§7.1: T2's spring flag) and item 113 (§7.7: the history replay at generation and the derivation rows for history). Item 112 names no §5-§7 home. ### Elegance candidates (for the canon to rule; not findings)

- DEL-01: The concern registry (§6.1) is a table in disguise: about 70 concerns across six prose paragraphs with per-concern attributes (geometry or not, rerollable, attempt cap, readers, keying, extra readers, retired). One table (id, kind, rerollable, cap, keyed by, readers, home) keeps every value and removes the narration of DIC-12. Nuance to keep: each concern's keying sentence as its cell; the retired ids reserved.
- DEL-02: F-EDIT's detection and action columns (§5.2) are a catalogue of about 25 refusal causes restated from §7.1, §7.3, §7.4, §7.5 and §7.7; a refusal-reason table (reason, detection, offered action, home) would be the one home the dry run, the lifecycle rows and the dossier contract cite. Nuance: each case's offered alternative differs and stays its own row.
- DEL-03: Rename { id, from, to } (§7.1) and ReportEntry's rename variant { id, from, to } (§5.4) are one shape; DisruptionReport.moved { use, from, to | null } and ReportEntry's relocate { use, from?, to? } are one family. Nuance: nullability of to differs (a vacate has no destination).
- DEL-04: The dossier-owned refusal is stated six times (DUPS) — one home (§7.7 item 11) with the others citing it.
- DEL-05: The "no undo target" rule for creation events, dossier events and epochs carrying one is stated in seven places — one home (§7.4 Replay semantics).
- DEL-06: One event -> tool binding table (DXC-04) instead of §7.1's partial table, §7.4's create-column tool names and V09 §4.3's table.
- DEL-07: The world-point records under a warp (§7.4, seven kinds in one long sentence with per-kind host rules, clearance and re-seat/orphan behaviour) as one table (record, host face, clearance, re-seat, orphan rule). Nuance: the door light's ring-edge fraction rule and Works' rings never re-seated.
- DEL-08: The schema transforms of §7.5 (two long paragraphs) as one table (record or field, older-document default, first-load derivation, source item). Nuance: "none is invented" cases stay rows.
- DEL-09: The §7.6 table enumeration (prose, about 60 tables) as a pointer to one registry (DXC-05) with readers, slice kinds and extension/presets columns.
- DEL-10: §5.3 rows that restate their rules' homes (epoch rows, Works, underground U1-U3, basement accesses, concealed institutions, clusters) could state only the check and scope and cite the rule's home. Nuance: the scope and the check columns are §5.3's own and stay.

### Edges observed without a stated outcome (input to the edge catalogue)

- A bug abort (F-NUM mandatory chain, F-PLAN ring refusal, F-PLAN count): outcome after the abort undefined (DWG-01).
- reroll.maxAdvance exhausted while building the three variations of §7.3 item 10: how many variations are shown (fewer than three? duplicates?) is not stated.
- New random on a scope entity (or a rung counter) already at the user cap 32 767: the next reroll is not stated (rerolls from 32 768 are reserved for faithfulness references).
- A warp session applied with no strokes (Apply on an empty session): whether an empty warp event is committed is not stated.
- drawBoundary whose polygon covers no L1 cell by at least 50 %: outcome (refused, bent to the nearest cell, empty district) not stated.
- setAttrMany whose query resolves to zero ids; roster with zero lines; group with zero quarters; fill or regenerate on an EditScope ring covering no face: outcomes not stated.
- A request kind with no DisruptionBudgetRow, or a budget of 0: outcome not stated.
- A dossier event that changes a value a user edit had already overridden as a map fact (e.g. a later dossier knownTo on a place whose knownTo the user set while it was a map fact): whether the user's value is superseded and listed as a fate is not stated.
- An undo section record larger than the 20 MB cap by itself: whether it is kept, dropped with a rebuild-by-replay, or withdrawn is not stated (the cap is stated for the section).

| id | where | concept, names used and hazard |
|---|---|---|
| DETERMINISM-0018 | §5.1 (6) | CONTENT DIGEST: the Merkle node of an entity's subtree (§6.2) computed with identity (address, lineage) and lifecycle left out — kinds, geometry and attributes only. Names used: "content digest". |
| DETERMINISM-0025 | §5 intro, §5.1, §5.2 | FAILURE LADDER / RUNG / TIER: a ladder is the declared ordered list of fallbacks of one failure class; a rung is one step of it; a tier (0-6) is the rung's kind in the seven-tier scale. Names used: "ladder", "fallback ladder" (F-LIGHT, §8.4), "rung", "tier", "ladder use". Note: "rung" is also the ladder-of-detail/elaboration rung (§2.9, item 28) and the population rung (TOWN…METROPOLIS) — three meanings of one word. — TERM-COLLISION: "rung" = failure-ladder step / elaboration rung / population rung |
| DETERMINISM-0082 | §5.3 deck row | HARBOUR WORK: a way of class pier or quay whose connected set of pier and quay edges meets land in at most one L0 land face (a pier, mole or breakwater arm rooted on one shore; a detached breakwater touching none, §4.8) — it stands in the water rather than crossing it; a pier or quay way joining two land faces across water crosses on a deck like any way; checked by one walk of connected pier/quay edges counting distinct L0 land faces (a few hundred edges at most). |
| DETERMINISM-0122 | §5.3 | BATCH-LOCAL / STAGE-END / SAVE / LOAD / CI-ONLY: the five invariant scopes (report encodes 'batch'\|'stage'\|'save'\|'ci'). Note: GenerationReport's scope enum has four values ('batch','stage','save','ci') while §5.3 names five scopes (load has no enum value). — ENUM-GAP: load scope absent from report enum |

| DETERMINISM-0124 | §5.2 F-EDIT, §7.7 | DOSSIER-OWNED value: a value the dossier supplied which no map event may change (refusal reason 'dossier-owned'): name or kind of a dossier-named element or quarter, knownTo, concealment, front or powers weights the dossier set, an assignment's state, a collective count outside its range, an adjust of a dossier-bound population or of a dossier-supplied slice value (§7.7 item 11; J-A20.77). |

| DETERMINISM-0165 | mechanism | §6.2 digest block | determinism | leaf(record) = H128(kind ‖ id ‖ lineage ‖ parent ‖ existence ‖ flags ‖ attributes in schema order ‖ digests of the faces and chains it owns); node(n) = H128(tag(n) ‖ children's digests sorted by address id); root = node(model) per layer: site (terrain raster, fields, L0 water); network (ways, junctions, walls, gates, towers); districts (district → block → plot → buildings, yards, households, pins, Passage records); hinterland; content (Allocation, FieldBands, institutions, factions, relations, Use records, Rejection records, MaterialLedger, Works records, LightSource records, staged ConditionOverlay, DepositSite and PowerRelation records — empty in the base); names and LabelEntity records; closeups (edited close-ups only); underground (UndergroundSystem, Tunnel, Gallery, Chamber, UndergroundLink, AccessPoint — plots under districts via the pseudo-district and undercity neighbourhoods); sky (SkyIsland records; districts under districts, arenas under plats); identity (tombstone ids, supersededBy links, lifecycle event block); plats (per arena and level, the kernel's level digests). | §2 records; §3.3 level digests | Snapshot model.digest (§2.9); goldens (§6.4); §6.5 (9) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0166 | mechanism | §6.2 | kernel §3.3 / determinism | Geometry hashed once by the kernel per level: each arena keeps a digest per level over its level-n route (vertices, roles, face owners and kinds of level ≤ n, never arena slots), updated along each commit record's dirty set (§3.3); finer Steiner vertices belong to the finer level's digest; per-level digests read each vertex's and edge's current birth level (promotion moves a vertex into the coarser digest, demotion back out; kept finer content keeps its leaves through a coarser re-derivation); an entity's leaf takes the digests of the faces and chains it owns (FaceRef, ChainRef), so no coordinate is hashed twice; coordinates in canonical order (level-n ring from its lowest-then-leftmost vertex of level ≤ n, finer vertices skipped); integers as 32-bit lanes, doubles as IEEE-754 bit patterns (−0 → +0; NaN refused by the schema), strings UTF-8; no text serialisation on the digest path; no byte-order dependence. | §3.3 commit record dirty set; §2.2 rule 3 promotion/demotion | DETERMINISM-0165 |  |

| DETERMINISM-0169 | mechanism | §6.2 Maintenance | determinism | Digest maintenance: at every batch commit the kernel re-hashes the leaves of touched records and the nodes on their paths to the root — O(touched × depth) — never by serialising the model (§3.3); the tree is built by the generation run in a session and, for a loaded document, by the load's one full recomputation from the decoded snapshot under the model packages of the document's own output version (§2.9 step 3; the snapshot stores only the root), so every edit maintains a complete tree; memory counted in §12.8's entity-store line; the canonical text serialisation (sorted keys, integer coordinates, floats as shortest round-trip strings) used only for golden files and exports. | §3.3; §2.9 step 3 | §12.8; §11 exports; goldens |  |
| DETERMINISM-0171 | mechanism | §6.2 records digest | determinism | Records digest: the persisted derivation and naming records (§2.8) form a second Merkle tree — leaf H128(id ‖ record kind ‖ fields in schema order ‖ each cited slice as (table, version, kind, key, hash)); one node per static-index partition (§3.3) plus one town node, each over its records sorted by address id; a root over the nodes; a record belongs to its entity's partition node (district, or the partition outside districts), records of entities belonging to no partition (ways, walls, water bodies, districts) to the town node; maintained like the model tree (a batch re-hashes each rewritten record's leaf and node; a batch moving an entity between nodes — re-parent across districts, district merge or split, outskirtToDistrict — moves its records' leaves and re-hashes both); root finalised when read, stored as recordsDigest, recomputed by every in-window load (§2.9 step 3) with the model tree; memory counted with the model tree's (§12.8); leaf encoding belongs to the file schema, not an output version (records are stored inputs), so any version checks it; a schema transform changing a record column (§7.5) checks the old root and writes the new. | §2.8 records; §3.3 partitions; §2.9 step 3 | Snapshot recordsDigest; §2.9 load; §7.5 transforms; replay = session and truncation identity (§6.5 (9), §7.3 item 7 (i)) |  |

| DETERMINISM-0176 | mechanism | §6.2 fallback (iii) | determinism (load §2.9) | Fallback (iii) full recomputation over 150 ms: the CI oracle recomputes per run one subtree per layer chosen by the run's seed (all covered across nightly runs) and compares with the maintained node; the load's recomputation leaves the edit path: a copy-on-write checkpoint of the decoded state at h₀ (§7.4 checkpoints), working state editable at once, model and records trees recomputed from the checkpoint in the generation worker between edits; each edit made meanwhile keeps its touched set (§7.4) and its leaves are re-hashed on the new trees in log order when it finishes; roots at h₀ compared with model.digest and model.recordsDigest as in §2.9 step 3; a mismatch is a corrupt cache — the replay's state replaces the working state at the head and the interim edits are re-applied in log order as "take the replayed town" does (§2.9 step 4), fates recomputed, differences reported; until roots match nothing is reported verified, "trust the saved town" and step 4's keep shown disabled ("checking the saved copy…"), and save, export, an API digest request and a bridged client's comparison with a rebase's stateDigest wait (edits do not); a mismatch withdraws the keep for good — a corrupt cache is never a base (J-DET-40, J-DET-45); a New random meanwhile computes its scope's content digests from the scope's own leaves (§5.1 (6)). | §7.4 checkpoints; §2.9 steps 3-4 | V09 application load UI; §11 bridge rebase |  |
| DETERMINISM-0177 | rule | §6.2 Histories, not states | determinism (dossier §7.7) | Histories, not states (item 22 (c); J-A22.71): the map is a function of the seed, the ordered history of dossier events and the map's own edits; replaying the same history reproduces the same map; two players reaching the same dossier by different histories may hold different equally valid maps (continuity chosen over path-independence; each dossier event is a least-disruption mutation of the map that stood, §7.7; the document keeps the whole history, §2.9). A dossier event's draws are keyed by the plots it touches and its own seq (§6.1, §6.3) — or, for a trade partner's or deposit's bearing, by that element's binding key — never by the dossier's values, so one history gives one digest on every host (§6.5 (9) history test). | DETERMINISM-0183 | table | §6.3 Lineage key | determinism | Derivation unit per kind (declared per stage in the registry): the town for S4 routes (destination order) and S6 districts (seed order); the growth stage for S5 ways (acceptance order); the L1 cell a way split, for new cells (split ordinal); the cell for S8 blocks and lanes; the block for S10 plots and alleys; the plot for S11 buildings, yard parts and households; the household for people (member ordinal); S6's run for Seam records (creation index by the run's lowest-then-leftmost vertex); the S5c growth run, the district's S8 run (baseLane portals) or the creating edit for portals; the parent surface district for an undercity neighbourhood (II.7.5; its district's lineage continues its parent's); the system for its galleries and chambers (II.7.6; part in the arena key); the connection stage's run for links (creation index by the pair's canonical order); the host for a light source (its light rule row and ordinal along the host as creation index, item 68); the target for a Works (start week as creation index, item 67); the creating edit event (seq) for anything a user draws or builds; otherwise (cells and junctions of a growth commit, walls, water bodies, bridges, greenery) the stage run that created it. Creation index = deterministic creation order of the unit's run or the stage's declared canonical order (faces and vertices: lowest-then-leftmost lattice point). | stage registry (§4.12, §14) | DETERMINISM-0182; V04 (undercity, systems); V06 (Works) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0185 | mechanism | §6.3 Address id | determinism | Address id = kind:token, prefixed <layer><part>/ outside the ground layer (the island's slot on the sky layer, the system's part on the underground layer, the sewer network's part 0 written bare: sky2/plot:…, under/tunnel:…, under3/gallery:…); a part number is part of the address and never reused (§3.4, J-A65.31); for a generated entity the token is the base-32 form of its lineage hash at creation (13 characters); for a user-created one the hash of the creating event's seq and its ordinal — closure pieces (§7.3 item 9, item 66) and sugar-expansion entities (§7.1, item 101) are user-created entities of their event, ordinals in the closure's dependency order and the expansion's order; the address is opaque: the parent is a field, never parsed from the id (J-DET-1). | DETERMINISM-0182; §3.4 parts; §7.1 seq | every reference in §2; §11 exports; §14 API |  |
| DETERMINISM-0186 | rule | §6.3 Never reused | determinism | Never reused; one collision rule: minting runs after succession matching; a fresh entity whose natural address equals a live address, a tombstone or an address its own batch retires takes suffix ~k with the smallest free k; a fresh lineage key equal to a live entity's outside the batch's re-derived set (a survivor, e.g. a locked plot) takes the smallest free dup component; entities the batch itself replaces are not collisions (so a faithful Regenerate (keep settings) re-mints their lineage keys, matches by anchor and reproduces them exactly); collisions resolved in the batch's canonical order (records sorted by lineage hash); an address an undo withdraws (§7.4) is free again but only the same derivation (same unit, lineage and reroll — a redo or repeated New random) can mint it. | DETERMINISM-0185; DETERMINISM-0189 matching | identity invariant (DETERMINISM-0091); §7.4 undo |  |
| DETERMINISM-0187 | rule | §6.3 Carry-over survival | determinism | Carry-over when a scope is re-derived (regenerate, Adjust, re-derive from Sk, a §7.3 local operator, template replacement, migration, an epoch): an entity not re-derived (outside the invalidation set, a fixed input — locked, pinned, edited or assigned §7.2 — or merely re-parented) survives: keeps address and lineage; only its parent field and a reparent lifecycle event change. Survivors in this sense: the kept parts of a designation mutation (§4.7 S11); rooms a stored-plan re-cut keeps along the interior ladder (item 28 (f)); the standing fabric of an epoch (vacant, derelict, ruined, cleared or reused building; neglected, breached or superseded enceinte; silted, caved-in or sealed tunnel; waning or settled island — state a lifecycle change); a reuse … build successor on a cleared trace succeeds at the trace's address (supersede); regrowth on an old plot re-uses the plot's address; raiseIsland mints the island's arena key and lineage from the town's lineage, slot index and epoch (item 8; J-A8.61); a use keeps its own address as it moves (anchor: plot address and role) while the building it leaves keeps its own (item 17); a mutation's added parts are user-created entities minted from the causing event's seq and ordinal; a dossier-backed assignment is a fixed input so its element survives every re-derivation of the ground under it (§7.7). | §7.2 fixed inputs; §4.11 epochs; II.6 islands | V06 evolution; V05 sky; §7.3 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0188 | rule | §6.3 Carry-over succession | determinism | A re-derived entity is matched to fresh entities by its anchor: the fresh entity of the same kind and class family whose anchor lies nearest within the kind's hysteresis radius succeeds it — takes the old address and the old generated name with its naming record (references re-bind; §7.3 item 4 decides which overrides move) and keeps its own fresh lineage (New random really re-rolls); a carry event records the lineage change and the same batch re-owns the successor's faces and roles under the address (setOwner, retypeEdges, §3.4); an old entity with no successor becomes a tombstone in the archive (§2.8) whose supersededBy names the fresh entity covering its anchor point, if any; a fresh entity with no predecessor keeps its fresh address; lifecycle events are written only for changes (existence, flags, lineage change, supersession, re-parenting), so an exact re-derivation leaves the model digest unchanged (J-DET-2). | DETERMINISM-0190 anchor table; §3.4 setOwner/retypeEdges; §2.8 archive | §7.3 item 4 override moves; §7.4 lifecycle; golden stability |  |
| DETERMINISM-0190 | rule | §6.3 Names | determinism (naming §9.6) | Names follow the address and their inputs: a generated name (text and short form) is handed to a successor with the address and its naming record (§2.8: naming slices its last draw consulted and its salt), as a lock or assignment is; S13a draws a name only in five cases: (1) an entity with no predecessor, town salt; (2) every unedited name under a town-wide rerollLayer(naming), new town salt; (3) entities an ids-scoped rerollLayer(naming) lists, each with salt one above the largest its name has been drawn with, advancing once more when equal to the old name up to reroll.maxAdvance, event storing salts used; (4) an unedited generated name whose naming inputs changed, with its record's salt — runs whenever S13a runs over the entity: after an adjust of the pack list (pack or corpus added/removed/selected/deselected, §7.6), an editTable or editPreset changing a naming slice, an upgrade's mapping (§7.5), a frozenImport rebase (§2.9 step 2, over every unedited name), the event ending the name's protection (unlock, unassign, undo/redo/revert removing the holding setAttr, lock or assign, §2.8 dormant record), any re-derivation reaching S13a; compares every cited slice with the current effective tables, a changed or unresolvable slice re-draws; (5) at an epoch, a district whose identity row changed at its character step (item 72): unedited generated name re-drawn on its naming stream with the renaming epoch's component and its record's salt, the old text moved to District.nameHistory (§2.5); a protected name (setAttr, lock, assignment, dossier naming claim, item 82) never re-drawn; listed in the epoch report (§5.4) and stored in EditBase.renames without confirmation (J-A72.62). | §2.8 naming record; §7.6 naming slices; §9.6 | S13a; §7.1 EditBase.renames; ReportEntry rename (DETERMINISM-0113) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0191 | rule | §6.3 Names | determinism (naming §9.6, §7.6) | Each field of a culture or ancestry row is its own naming slice (§7.6) and a name's record cites only the fields its draw read, so selecting another corpus for one field re-draws exactly the names from that field; a carried name is kept exactly while its naming inputs are unchanged; a change of the features a grammar guard reads (a district that lost its gate) is not a naming input and renames nothing (§9.6); only case (5) renames a district at an epoch and only past its identity's hysteresis; every draw uses the entity's naming stream (keyed from its entity key with the case's salt and the attempt) and writes the naming record anew. | §7.6 slices; §9.6 grammar guards | S13a |  |
| DETERMINISM-0192 | rule | §6.3 Names | determinism | Renames of case (4) are confirmed like fates: the dry run lists each (old and new text) in the edit's fate report, the edit commits after the user confirms (an action whose dry run lists any returns confirm, a frozenImport rebase included), the confirmed renames stored in EditBase.renames (§7.1); a replay or receiving client re-draws identically; one computing different renames reports a mismatch under §7.3 item 4's rule; a user-set name (setAttr), a locked name path and an assigned name are never re-drawn while protected, each keeping its naming record dormant for the event ending the protection (§2.8); when carrying or a re-draw brings two equal names into one uniqueness scope (§9.6, e.g. a district merge joining two lanes of the same name) the entity whose address sorts later re-draws on its own stream and the rename is listed in the fate report (J-DET-37, J-DET-46). | §7.1 dry run confirm; §7.3 item 4 | V09/V10 confirm dialogs; §9.6 uniqueness |  |
| DETERMINISM-0193 | rule | §6.3 Anchors and hysteresis | determinism | Matching is by distance, never by quantised cell: candidate (old, fresh) pairs within the radius and of the same class family are sorted by (distance, old id, fresh id) and assigned one-to-one greedily in that order. | DETERMINISM-0194 | DETERMINISM-0197 matching routine |  |
| DETERMINISM-0194 | table | §6.3 anchor table | determinism | Anchor table (kind: anchor; radius; class family): plot — highest-class frontage (way address, start offset along the way); 0.5 m along the same way or its successor; use family (residential, commercial, institutional, agricultural). building — footprint centroid; 0.5 m; building class family (table row). block, cell — centroid; 2 m (block), 5 m (cell). district — seed feature's address; same feature; district kind family. way — end junctions' addresses, else overlap ≥ 50 % of length; way class family. junction, gate, tower, pin — position; 1 m (junction, pin), 2 m (gate, tower); kind. bridge — deck midpoint on the same river (water body's address or successor); crossing.searchBandM (default 120 m, §4.2 S1 (5)); kind (bridge, ford). enceinte, water body, greenery, land use — overlap ≥ 50 % (IoU); kind. household, person, label — (plot address, ordinal); (household address, ordinal); target address. use — (plot address, role), a relocation moves it by its event (item 17); use class family. room of a stored plan — centroid within its wing per storey; 0.5 m in the wing's frame; any role (item 28 (f)). entrance ('entrance', building, i, §2.2 rule 7) — carried with its wing by carryWings (§2.6), re-matched by (wing, side, offset); half the side's length; entrance role. passage (item 30) — (front plot address, kerb end midpoint); 0.5 m along the same kerb; one family. access point (II.7.4, items 12, 64) — (element's address, position of its ('access_point', id) vertex); 1 m; AccessPoint.kind. tunnel, gallery — end junctions else overlap ≥ 50 % in own part; Tunnel.kind / system kind. chamber — floor face centroid in own part; 2 m; system kind. underground link — its two ends' element addresses; same pair; UndergroundLink.via. undercity neighbourhood — parent surface district's address and void system's part. light source — (host address, light rule row, ordinal along host); 1 m from recorded point; light kind. Works — (target address, start week). condition overlay, deposit site, power relation (item 88, staged, empty in the base) — binding key (§7.7 item 12). sky island — its slot key (raiseIsland; J-A8.61). seam (NF-P6) — its two districts' addresses and first chain's address. portal — position; 1 m; portal kind (baseLane, growth, edit). | §2 record kinds; §4.2 S1 (5) crossing.searchBandM | DETERMINISM-0197 matching routine; V02, V03 (rooms), V04 (underground kinds), V05 (islands), V06 (Works), V08 (binding keys) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0195 | rule | §6.3 after table | determinism | Split through an anchor: the child left of the directed cut keeps the id (§3.6 (5)); a household's address survives a re-derivation of its plot by succession (plot address and ordinal), so a person's key (household address, member ordinal, birth epoch, §10.3) is stable wherever the household is; when S1's water operator re-sites an edited reach's crossings (§7.3 (b) 2), each old deck is succeeded by the re-sited deck nearest its old midpoint within the band, keeping name, assignment and its way's reference. | §3.6 (5); §10.3 person key | §7.3 (b) 2 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0196 | bar | §6.3 Measurement | determinism | Measurement (M1, B-EDIT-lite): 1 cm perturbations of every vertex keep ≥ 99.9 % of addresses; pre-decided fallback: building and plot radii double to 1 m and an IoU ≥ 0.5 test joins the class-family check. | DETERMINISM-0194 | §16 M1; §13.3 B-EDIT-lite |  |
| DETERMINISM-0197 | mechanism | §6.3 matching routine | determinism | One matching routine hands addresses on in every re-derivation (scoped regeneration, Adjust, Re-derive from Sk — from S1 the whole town, a local operator, template replacement, epochs, migration, the upgrade mapping of §7.5): point-anchored kinds (plots by frontage offset, buildings, blocks, cells, junctions, gates, towers, pins, bridges, passages by kerb end, access points) through a uniform grid per (kind, class family) with cell = hysteresis radius, candidates in the 3 × 3 neighbourhood, pairs sorted by (distance, old id, fresh id) and assigned greedily; overlap-anchored kinds (ways and tunnels without matched end junctions, enceintes, water bodies, greenery, land use) take candidates from the kernel's spatial index (§3.3) and compute overlap on those only; a sky island matched by its slot and a staged dossier record by its binding key, no distance read. | DETERMINISM-0193-DETERMINISM-0194; §3.3 spatial index | §7.3 every operator; §7.5 upgrade; §4.11 epochs |  |
| DETERMINISM-0198 | number | §6.3 matching budget | determinism | Matching budget ≤ 2 µs per re-derived point-anchored entity and ≤ 20 µs per overlap-anchored one (median), included in §12.6's scope costs; measured (Apple M1, Node 24; docs/validation/bench/fixall_det_r2_match.out): 0.60 µs per entity for a 450-building district regeneration, 0.59 µs for a 30 000-entity CITY re-derivation (0.38 µs when unrelated), 0.59 µs for an 80 000-entity METROPOLIS mapping at 2 m radius; measurement M0 (d) then M1 on B-EDIT-lite and a Re-derive-from-S1 fixture; pre-decided fallback: matching per static-index partition (district first, leftovers across partitions) — a declared shift (§6.4) since a boundary entity can take a farther in-district candidate. | DETERMINISM-0197 | §12.6; §16 M0 (d), M1 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0199 | rule | §6.3 JOIN and SPLIT | determinism | JOIN and SPLIT (item 31 (c); J-A31.73): a joined plot takes its address by the anchor rule from the member whose frontage anchor it keeps; other members become tombstones with supersededBy = the joined plot, members is history; its lineage continues the lineage key of its largest member by area (ties by address id) — the exception to J-DET-2 — so its buildings' and yard parts' streams continue; the epoch component keeps an epoch's re-cut distinct (owner: modifications of the original seed, not new rolls). A SPLIT keys each child by the parent's lineage key with the child's index as creation index; the child keeping the parent's frontage anchor keeps the address, the others take fresh addresses; every child takes the continued lineage; a later SPLIT resurrects a member whose anchor matches — the one permitted reuse of a tombstoned id, recorded as a resurrect event (safe since a JOIN's member ids were never given to another entity) — taking its address from the tombstone, lineage from the parent and child index, builtBand (§2.5) from the rung-3 event that re-cut it, never from the tombstone's last record; only an undo restores that record exactly (§7.4). | §7.1 join/split; §4.11 rung 3; §2.5 builtBand | §5.3 mutation row (DETERMINISM-0068); §7.4 undo |  |
| DETERMINISM-0200 | rule | §6.3 JOIN and SPLIT | determinism | A tunnel record split by a stretch conversion (II.6, J-A11.43) follows the split rule with the centreline's first vertex as frontage anchor (part holding it keeps the address, other parts minted from the causing event's seq and ordinal, every part continues the tunnel's lineage with its part index); a block's Frontage record (§2.5) the same split cuts at a stretch end follows the same rule (part holding the record's first kerb edge in centreline order keeps the address; every other part — two where the record spans a whole interior stretch — minted from seq and ordinal; each continues the lineage with its part index; an undo restores the one record, §7.4); a mutation continues lineage; only a re-derivation takes a fresh one (New random and every successor matched in a re-derived scope: Adjust, Re-derive from Sk, §7.3 local operator, template replacement, migration, what an epoch re-derives) (J-DET-2, amended so). | II.6 stretch conversion; §2.5 Frontage | V04 (tunnels), V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0201 | rule | §6.3 Dossier bindings | determinism (dossier §7.7) | Dossier bindings never re-key (item 74): a dossier re-binding re-points a binding row of Document.dossier and never re-keys a map address (records keep ids, lineage, geometry); a dossier name change carrying no receipt is a removal plus an addition (§7.7), and the addition mints nothing until the solver places the new element, from its placing event's seq. | Document.dossier (§7.7) | V08 SettlementForge |  |
| DETERMINISM-0202 | rule | §6.3 Document identity | determinism | docId (a UUID) minted when a document is created, kept on save, re-minted on clone and save-as; addresses are document-scoped (a clone keeps them); at the API and bridge boundary every id is rendered <docId>/<id>; exports carry addresses and a supersedes table (tombstone → successor). | §2.9 Document | §11 exports; §14 API; V08 host |  |
| DETERMINISM-0203 | rule | §6.3 Workers | determinism | Workers merge results in lineage order, never arrival order. | DETERMINISM-0182 | §12 workers; DETERMINISM-0161 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0204 | rule | §6.3 scope of stability (O2 b, e) | determinism | Scope of stability: (1) independent concerns — adding or changing a concern changes no other concern's draws; a naming input change re-draws only the names citing it; (2) scoped rerolls — New random changes nothing outside its effective scope (§7.3); (3) upstream changes — a change to stage k's code or tables changes stage k's outputs and what derives from them: a declared cascade shift (§6.4, §13.3), never silent; stages upstream of k and independent of it stay byte-identical; (4) edited documents — stability across such changes comes from carry-over by anchor and migration (§7.5), never from stream keys. | §6.1 concerns; §7.3; §6.4 shifts; §7.5 | O2 (b, e); §13.3 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0205 | test | §6.3 Measurement (M0 d and M1) | determinism | Identity fixtures: minting and matching within budget; B-EDIT fixtures for drawBoundary, district merge and split, outskirtToDistrict, regenerate around a lock, a course-knot move that re-sites a bridge (bridge keeps address and name), New random on a district (matched successors keep names; only entities without a predecessor draw one), a district merge joining two equal lane names (later address re-draws, reported), the recycling case (a superseded assigned building whose creation slot is reused) — 0 duplicate ids and 0 silent re-binds; naming-input fixtures are §13.2's. | DETERMINISM-0186-DETERMINISM-0199 | §13.2; §16 M0 (d), M1 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0206 | rule | §6.4 Version axes | determinism | Output version (o<k>): the observable output of the model packages; bumps exactly when a release declares a shift (any stage digest moves on the golden suite, §13.3); a release changing no output keeps it (documents, links, goldens stay valid); every document and link names the output version it replays under. | §13.3 golden suite | Recipe/Document outputVersion (§2.9); links (DETERMINISM-0213); §7.5 upgrade; GenerationReport.outputVersion |  |
| DETERMINISM-0207 | rule | §6.4 Version axes | determinism | Generator version = the code build, informational (bug reports), never a replay key; schema version = the file format (§7.5). | build | bug reports; §7.5 schema transforms |  |
| DETERMINISM-0208 | rule | §6.4 Preview flag | determinism / scope §16 | Preview flag: links and documents made before output version 1 (fixed at M4's exit, §16) carry preview: true (in a link &pv=1) and the app shows §13.3's preview notice; the flag disappears from output version 1 on. | §16 M4 exit | §13.3 preview notice; V09 application |  |
| DETERMINISM-0209 | rule | §6.4 Table versions | determinism (§7.6) | Table versions: each rule table separately, pinned in the recipe; a table change that changes output is part of an output-version bump; a user override carries its base row's hash (§7.6). | §7.6 table pins | Recipe table pins; §7.5 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0210 | rule | §6.4 Pack versions | determinism (§2.9 pack set) | Pack versions: each setting pack by id, version and content hash, in the recipe or in the adjust event that adds it (§2.9's pack set); shipped packs belong to the output version like its tables (each retained version's chunk carries its own); a non-shipped pack's content travels in the document; a pack a later version stops shipping is copied into the document by the upgrade to that version (§7.5); a selected user corpus is a non-shipped pack user:<content hash> (§2.9). | §2.9 pack set | §7.5 upgrade copy; F-EDIT cap case; V11 content |  |

| DETERMINISM-0215 | rule | §6.4 Permalinks | determinism (UI text V09) | Otherwise the app says which condition failed ("this town uses the setting pack X, which is not built in"; "this town uses your own name list X" — also when deselected; "this town uses content from the host program Y"; "this town was kept from a saved copy and cannot be rebuilt from a link"; "too many edits for a link") and offers the .plat file, or &doc=<ref> when the host configured a document resolver with publish (DocResolver, §11.5); the document carries pack content and host outputs (§2.9); preset=<id> never appears — an edited preset elsewhere cannot change the town (J-DET-50, J-DET-17, J-DET-35). | DETERMINISM-0214; §11.5 DocResolver | V09 application; V11 content (message keys) |  |

| DETERMINISM-0229 | rule | §6.5 (7) | determinism / kernel §3.7 | One boolean and offset engine per output version on every host (D-14): no host may silently run a different engine; changing the engine is an output-version bump with a declared shift. | §3.7 | DETERMINISM-0161; DETERMINISM-0237 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0230 | rule | §6.5 (8) | determinism | Host providers: a host-supplied table arrives as an editTable, editPreset or setStyle event whose content is copied into the log with provenance host (§7.6); host code registered as a provider (ContentProvider, §14) passes a double-run check at registration (same request twice in two fresh workers returns identical digests); its results are recorded in the document's hostContent block keyed by (provider id, version, input hash) and read from there on replay (§2.9, §11.4), so the document replays without the host; setting packs follow the same rule through the packs block (§2.9). | §14 ContentProvider; §2.9 hostContent, packs | V08 host; §11.4; F-EDIT cap |  |

| DETERMINISM-0311 | rule | §7.1 type note | determinism | Ownership note: Designation and MutationRecord are §4.7 S11's, RoomRole and Entrance §2.6's, ConversionOp §4.11's; Works, LightSource and UndergroundLink §2.7's (rules §4.11's, §4.7's, II.7.6's); AccessPoint II.7.4's; DossierChange §7.7's; ClosureRecord §7.3 item 9's. |  | reference map |  |
| DETERMINISM-0312 | rule | §7.1 | determinism | No event stores a VertexId, HalfEdgeId or FaceId (§2.2 rule 5): geometry is addressed by entity address, by lattice point with a role signature (bound by resolveVertexAt, never by distance alone), by a water body's address with a course-knot ordinal (§7.4's moveCourseKnot row), or by a ring; undo and redo are events like the rest (§7.4): the log only grows, which lets a host store it (§11.3). | §2.2 rule 5; resolveVertexAt | §7.4; §11.3 host log |  |
| DETERMINISM-0313 | rule | §7.1 named actions | determinism | The named actions (G-R4; O16-core) are separate operations with distinct semantics, never one flag. Regenerate (keep settings): re-derives the scope with each entity's recorded inputs and unchanged rerolls (§7.3): faithful, byte-identical when nothing upstream changed; used after a lock or a drawn constraint. | §7.3 recorded inputs | V09 UI; regenerate mode keep |  |
| DETERMINISM-0314 | rule | §7.1 named actions | determinism | New random — always on a scope (a block, a quarter, a building's rung, a person, or a rerollable layer such as the trees via rerollLayer); an unscoped new town is only the empty state's action before anything is declared (§7.3 item 10, item 101); advances the reroll of every scope entity by one, written as an absolute map (replay exact; blocks at different rerolls each move on); an unchanged scope entity advances again under §5.1 (6)'s check. | §5.1 (6); §6.1 reroll | V09 UI; regenerate mode newRandom |  |
| DETERMINISM-0315 | rule | §7.1 named actions | determinism | A designation change is neither: assign, unassign, a setAttr on a plot's use or class, a relocated use and a dossier event run §4.7 S11's mutation of the standing design toward the new designation's template (kept parts kept, additions and subtractions recorded) — never a re-derivation, never a re-roll (§7.3 item 8, item 18); a click on a stale elaboration rung re-derives it the same way from its canonical prior (§7.3's table: the stored plan, else the as-built plan carried along §2.6's interior ladder — never the cache; item 28, item 28 (f)). | §4.7 S11 mutation; §7.3 item 8 | V02, V03, V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0316 | rule | §7.1 named actions | determinism (§7.8 registry) | Adjust re-solves with a changed parameter; locality promised only for parameters the control registry (§7.8) lists as consumed at S10 or later or that carry a spatial scope (a district's density, a block's granularity); a parameter consumed earlier (seed, rung, water setting and side, walls, castle option, gate count, planned ↔ organic, wealth level, the recipe's pack list) has no local operator and is offered as Re-derive from stage k: everything from Sk down re-derives with carry-over (§6.3) after the user confirms the edit-fate report. | §7.8 control registry | adjust event; V09 UI |  |
| DETERMINISM-0317 | rule | §7.1 named actions | determinism | Same kind, new seed is Enter's successor (new document with seed + 1 keeping the recipe and the dossier, §6.1); per district (PART II) it is New random on the district; Surprise me is a new document whose intent is drawn from the intent.surprise stream of a fresh seed and written into its recipe (§7.7). | DETERMINISM-0156; intent.surprise concern | V09 UI; §7.7 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0318 | mechanism | §7.1 Warp | determinism | Warp is MFCG's staged edit: strokes in a warp session preview on the L1 overlay at interactive rate (vertex moves and a redraw of affected tiles, no invariants, §8.8); Apply commits one warp event holding the strokes in order (committed only on Apply); Discard drops them; on Apply and every replay each stroke is applied as its operation to the geometry current at that log position, never as stored displacements: the operation computes a displacement field over the vertices under the brush and the kernel's warp(field, fromLevel, region) applies it with §3.6's whole-step validation, halving, re-seating, sliding and held vertices (the dry run lists held entities), re-deriving locally any face then failing a class floor, logged; point-standing records move with their host face and every moved footprint, trace and deck is re-based (§7.4); a fromLevel = 0 warp moving water hands the moved shores to S1's water operator as its new water (§7.3 (b) 1). | §3.6 warp; §8.8 preview | §7.4 warp row; §7.3 (b) 1; V09 tools |  |
| DETERMINISM-0319 | table | §7.1 warp kernel table | determinism | Warp kernels vs MFCG tools: displace (drags vertices by dragCm: full weight inside innerRadiusCm, falling linearly to 0 at radiusCm); liquify (displace with a smooth cosine falloff over the whole radius); relax (Laplacian relaxation toward equal edge lengths); bloat (each L1 cell under the brush grows about its centroid by strength, neighbours yielding); pinch (each L1 cell shrinks about its centroid); equalize (each cell moves its vertices strength × 20 % toward regularFit's targets, §3.4 — MFCG's tool is its castle routine, §4.8, item 45); Rotate → the rotation view key (§2.9): whole-map rotation about the frame centre as view state, never a vertex operation; model north (+y) unchanged; compass view key trueNorth (default) or free; exports apply rotation per §11.1 (J-DET-15); Measure → the measure tool of §9.5, view data, no model event; swirl (our local rotation about the brush centre); jitter (seeded random displacement from warp.jitter). | §3.4 regularFit; warp.jitter concern | warp event; §11.1; §9.5 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0320 | number | §7.1 warp table | determinism | equalize moves vertices strength × 20 % toward targets per application. |  | DETERMINISM-0319 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0321 | rule | §7.1 Drawing at the authoring boundary | determinism | Pointer samples resampled along arc length to spacing ≥ minEdge, simplified with a topology-preserving Visvalingam at minEdge; a self-crossing stroke split into simple runs before insertChain (§3.4); drawWater's add rings unioned by the boolean engine and subtract rings a land brush (island = subtract ring inside an add ring; overlapping sea shapes union — FTG's method); rivers and canals take a line and width; drawBoundary selects L1 cells whose area lies ≥ 50 % inside the drawn polygon, the district boundary becoming those cells' boundary; the dry run shows the snapped boundary before commit (J-DET-14); a drawWay of class bridge across a river face places its Bridge by §4.2 S1 (5)'s drawn-bridge rule, S4's operator routing the ends to its deck (§7.3). | §3.4 insertChain; minEdge (§3.2) | drawWater/drawBoundary/drawWay; V09 canvas |  |
| DETERMINISM-0322 | rule | §7.1 Building-level editing | determinism | drawFootprint places a hand-drawn footprint (concave allowed, simple) on the plot containing it — spanning several plots proposes their JOIN in the dry run; on unplotted land (green, residue, field) creates a plot around it with access to the nearest way, or returns a conflict if none reachable — the host plot's site plan re-fitted around it as a fixed slot (buildings no longer fitting listed in the fate report); build places a building of a class on a plot (site plan picks the footprint when none given) — §4.11's build(...); moveFootprint moves or turns a footprint, leaving its plot re-homes it with both site plans re-fitted; swapUse exchanges two buildings' uses, households and assignments atomically, footprints staying (two relocateUse conversions under one event, item 17); a use is opened, closed or relocated by hand via convert openUse/closeUse/relocateUse, the building persisting vacant or converted; fill forces an arrangement on the scope's plots and re-runs S11 there. | §4.7 S11; §4.11 | edit kinds DETERMINISM-0266-DETERMINISM-0270 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0323 | rule | §7.1 Entrances, basements, passages and plans | determinism | setEntrance adds, moves or removes a side, rear, yard or service door under §2.6's rules and never removes the main entrance (movable only along the §2.6 rule 1 side); setAttr(basement) overrides the derived basement decision (an edited attribute); drawPassage reserves a covered passage face (§2.5) giving the plots behind their access rows; convert sealPassage/openPassage sets Passage.sealed; Passage.headroomCm and Passage.frontBuilding are derived (S11 writes them with the spanning wings' spans; a heights edit of a spanning wing re-derives the headroom; the event making the front building a footprint trace or tombstone clears frontBuilding) and have no edit kind. | §2.5 Passage; §2.6 BaseWing.spans | edit kinds DETERMINISM-0278-DETERMINISM-0279 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0324 | rule | §7.1 knownTo | determinism (§7.7) | setAttr(knownTo) hides or reveals a place's name and purpose for §9.2's label policy; a knownTo the dossier set is dossier-owned and a map setAttr of it is refused (F-EDIT dossier-owned; §7.7 items 7, 11; J-A20.77), while a label's display text and visibility (§9.1) and a viewer's knowledge key (§11.3) remain map-side choices (item 30); a front's concealed programme (item 63) is edited through setEntrance and the plan events. | §9.2 label policy; §11.3 | F-EDIT; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0325 | rule | §7.1 plan events | determinism / V03 interiors | The plan events edit a building's stored plan storey by storey, the first storing the composition (§2.6), each dry-run against §13.2's plan bars on the overlay, so a move leaving a room below its row's minimum or unreachable, or a unit without its own door (item 28 (f)), returns F-EDIT, never a silent repair; a building's household count is changed by hand through convert relet (§4.11) within §2.6's capacity, marking an unstored plan stale and re-cutting a stored one inside the event (§7.3); an edit lowering a building's capacity below its households re-homes the excess by §2.6's capacity rule, each move among its fates (§7.3 item 4); a click on a building or neighbourhood logs nothing (item 28 (c)). | §2.6 capacity; §13.2 plan bars | F-EDIT; F-PLAN; V03 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0326 | rule | §7.1 Brushes | determinism | paint sets roof kind, roof colour or wall colour on every building whose footprint centroid lies under the brush path (ids resolved at dry run and stored); paintGround records a ground-cover region in the style (pixels only); the tree brush is a batch of instance events on the entities under the brush (§8.10), one undo step; painting trees where no green entity exists first logs a drawGreen (a wood on open land, a yard_planting in a yard); instance events also carry close-up presentation edits (II.4); field brushes are setField strokes (§7.2); the terrain brush's water mode chooses locked (default) or follow. | §8.10; II.4 | paint/instance/setField events; V09 brushes |  |
| DETERMINISM-0327 | rule | §7.1 People | determinism / pipeline §10 | A lock with paths fixes chosen attributes of a person (name, age, role …); New random on a person re-derives it with a new reroll keeping locked paths; relations and schedules are not re-derived by a person reroll (town-wide, §10); setAttrMany edits many entities at once (job, workplace, schedule, household relocation) from a query resolved to ids at dry run and stored (replay never re-runs the query), one undo step. | §10 people | setAttrMany; lock paths |  |
| DETERMINISM-0328 | rule | §7.1 Edit targets (owner amendment 5) | determinism | Every generated element is editable; every rule table and preset is editable (§7.6), by a user through the preference layer and by us through new table versions; edits are first-class events that persist (the log, §2.9), undo (§7.4; undo/redo are log events so they work in a shared document), survive regeneration (§7.2-§7.3) and migrate (§7.5); the generator repairs around them locally (§7.3, F-REDERIVE); the preference and override layer sits above the defaults (§7.6); the entity registry (§2.8) declares each type's edit kinds with their undo path and lifecycle row; its completeness test is §13.2's (§2.8). | owner amendment 5; §2.8 entity registry | §13.2 registry completeness test |  |
