| DETERMINISM-0341 | table-row | §7.1 edit targets: terrain and water conversions | determinism / V06 evolution | terrain and water conversions (§4.11, item 25): convert (reclaim, siltReach, shiftCourse, digCanal — the slow conversions the evolution engine will schedule, usable by hand now), repaired through S1's terrain or water operator or the carve (a marsh's reclaim by the terrain operator §7.3 (a); a foreshore's reclaim, siltReach and shiftCourse by the water operator §7.3 (b); digCanal by the carve §4.11); derived, no edit kind: LandUse.reclaimed (written by reclaim, §2.3). | §4.11 conversions; §7.3 (a)-(b) | V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0342 | table-row | §7.1 edit targets: works, lights | determinism | works (§4.11, item 67; J-A67.71): startWorks, cancelWorks, setAttr on the Works (startWeek, its duration within its WorksTable row) — its stage at a week derived, never set. lights (§2.7, item 68; J-A68.72): placeLight, setAttr (kind, heightCm, colour, intensity, radiusCm; point — re-hosted by pointLocate on its host's PlatKey, refused as F-EDIT off its host), delete, lock; derived lights (lit windows, the plan rung's room lights) have no edit kind; the view's light keys (§2.9) are not logged. | §4.11 Works; §2.7 LightSource | V06; §8.4 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0343 | table-row | §7.1 edit targets: greenery, fields, names | determinism | greenery, trees: drawGreen, tree brush, instance, setAttr, delete. fields: setField (prosperity, density, greenery); setAttr on the town's FieldBands (an explicit re-banding) — both authorial: the blocks they re-derive past τ take the new field as their built state and their plots' builtBand (§7.2, item 31). names, labels: setAttr (text; a label's displayText, §9.1, cleared by revert), moveLabel, hideLabel, rerollLayer(naming) town-wide or on chosen ids, group (region label group RegionGroup, §9.1, sugar, item 101; renamed by setAttr, removed by delete); a name the dossier gave or claimed is dossier-owned, its label's display text a map-side choice (item 20 (h)). | §7.2 field brushes; §9.1 | V01 fields |  |
| DETERMINISM-0344 | table-row | §7.1 edit targets: layers, people, close-ups, tables, history | determinism | layers, style: setLayer, setStyle, paintGround; view keys are not logged (§2.9). people, households: setAttr, setAttrMany, lock with paths, regenerate (person scope), pin; a household's building and dwelling unit (§2.7, item 28 (f)) follow relet and item 17's relocation (§4.11, §10.3), never a bare setAttr. close-ups (stage 2): every kind above on close-up entities after closeup; instance for presentation edits. rule tables, presets: editTable, editPreset (§7.6). history: epoch, convert, rebase, upgrade, undo, redo; the dossier events — assign, unassign and setAttr with EditBase.dossier and EditBase.dossierChange set, produced by the dossier port and folded in the host's order (§7.7, items 73, 75); the creation prefix's assign events carry EditBase.creation (§7.4). | §2.9 view keys; §10.3 | V01; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0345 | mechanism | §7.1 Dry-run validation (G-R10) | determinism | Every event is validated before commit on the scratch overlay: the schema, the batch-local and stage-end invariants of its scope (§5.3), and the edit-fate report of its effective scope with its case-(4) renames (§7.3 item 4); for a chained operator (§7.3 item 3) the whole chain runs on the overlay so the report lists every re-sited bridge, re-routed or cut way and re-based building before commit; for a declaration's points form the derived shape with its handles; for an area declaration (quarter, fill, roster) the plots, households and buildings it will hold — the count the ghost's badge shows (§9.5; item 101 R14); for undo or redo, §7.4's independence check; conflicts come back as actions (F-EDIT), never silently resolved; a warp that would break an invariant is halved and reported. | §5.3; §7.3 item 3-4; §7.4 independence | every edit; §9.5 ghost badge; V09/V10 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0346 | rule | §7.1 Dry-run validation | determinism | An E_KEPT conflict (the edit would cross or clear finer content an earlier stage made, or content outside a coarse edit's clear region, §3.3) is offered with one resolution: start the re-derivation at the stage that made that content (for a wall's band parts or a margin band's part, S10's step (0) for the wall strips of the touched run or for the scope's side of the touched stretch, §4.7, §7.3 item 2); a host's bridge commands map onto these events (§11.3). | §3.3 E_KEPT | §11.3 bridge; F-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0347 | rule | §7.1 Dry-run validation | determinism (§7.7) | A dossier event or positive placement is dry-run like any edit: its disruption (every moved use, joined or vacated plot and mutated building, each with the causing rule) computed against the request kind's budget row (§7.7) and stored in EditBase.disruption; a dossier event over budget still lands (dossier authority absolute) and is reported over budget; a user's positive placement over budget, or one no plot satisfies inside the hard rows, returns F-EDIT with the closest acceptable sites (§4.7 S10b); a join or split of plots that would remove an occupied building returns F-EDIT "vacate first" (item 17's relocation) unless the user confirms the demolition as a fate (item 31); a map event without EditBase.dossier that would close, remove or re-designate a dossier-backed element returns F-EDIT with rejectSite and placeAt offered (J-A20.75); a map event changing a dossier-owned value returns F-EDIT dossier-owned with the map-side alternative named where there is one (§5.2). | §7.7 budget rows | F-EDIT (DETERMINISM-0051) | restates F-EDIT cases of §5.2 (double statement within unit) |
| DETERMINISM-0348 | rule | §7.1 authoring toolkit (item 101; J-A101.71) | determinism (tools surfaced by V09/V10) | The authoring toolkit (owner: "come up with an elegant set of tools for both an ai clerk and a user where a dedicated user can build it or emulate it"): declare, then derive — authoring is a set of DECLARATIONS, each one of the events above or sugar over one, with one dry run, one fate report, one commit and one undo; the rules derive everything undeclared (item 20 (h) applied to human input): a declared fact stays fixed like a dossier fact until its author changes it (§7.2; §2.8's factTier reads it declared); a declaration is refused only where it contradicts a dossier fact or a lock (reason and closest acceptable declarations, item 20 (g); F-EDIT); every other infeasible declaration is bent by its dry run to the nearest lawful one, named in one line (item 101 R2); emulating a city = declaring intent, bones and programme (L0-L2), building it = declaring the fabric too (L3-L4). | item 101; §2.8 factTier | V09 application; V10 clerk; §13.2 elegance bars |  |
| DETERMINISM-0349 | table | §7.1 twelve tools | determinism (tool home; UI surfaces V09, V10) | The twelve tools in five layers (author may stop at any layer; everything below derived): L0 intent — T1 intend (the recipe's intent at creation, §7.7 layer 1: site, size, form, era and style, prosperity, walls; afterwards the Adjust and Re-derive events of the basic panel's controls, its sliders, §7.8); L1 ground and bones — T2 ground (setField(terrain), warp, drawWater (points and level forms), moveCourseKnot), T3 wall (drawWall points form, placeGate), T4 road (drawWay with class; across water a bridge; points form), T5 quarter (quarter); L2 programme — T6 place (placeAt point form, assign, build, drawFootprint), T7 roster (roster); L3 fabric — T8 fill (fill, setField density/greenery/prosperity, drawGreen; its three variations §7.3 item 10), T9 shape (split, join, drawFootprint, moveFootprint, setAttr, setEntrance, paint, the plan events), T10 hold (lock, pin, revert, regenerate keep or New random on a scope); L4 finish — T11 name (setAttr(name), moveLabel, hideLabel, group), T12 dress (setStyle, setLayer, paintGround; furniture §9.5, exports §11; the underlay is view state). | edit kinds DETERMINISM-0252-DETERMINISM-0307 | V09 canvas; V10 clerk tool API; §11.1 skeleton; §13.2 | WIRING-GAP-CANDIDATE: edit kinds with no tool row (see md) |
| DETERMINISM-0350 | rule | §7.1 toolkit | determinism | A new capability adds a parameter to an existing tool, never a thirteenth tool (§13.2's elegance bars). | DETERMINISM-0349 | §13.2 elegance bars |  |
| DETERMINISM-0351 | mechanism | §7.1 Sugar events (J-A101.72) | determinism | Sugar events are logged as themselves (one undo step, one fate report) and expanded at their fold into the events they stand for by the sugar expansion table (sugar.json, §7.6) at the version the event records (expansion), so replay and upgrade expand exactly as the session did (§7.5); what an expansion creates is minted from the sugar event's seq (§6.3). placeGate(enceinte, at) = a short through-way stub of the enceinte's gate class crossing the trace at at, square to it, so S7's operator builds the gate (a gate also arises wherever a declared road crosses a wall, §7.3's S7 row); quarter = a drawBoundary (kind's size band and snap), a whole-neighbourhood assign naming that kind as its function (read by §4.5's character as a dossier kind, a declared kind winning over an emergent identity), a setAttr(name) and, when a band is given, a setField(prosperity) stroke moving the boundary's mean to the band's centre; roster = one assign per line (class, name, locus as PlacementCriteria hints) in one group solved together by §4.7 S10b's solver in one dry run in claims-first order, a line the rules cannot honour listed with its closest options and not committed; with a dossier connected a line must name a dossier element (RosterLine.element) and expands to its placeAt, a line naming none returns F-EDIT dossier-owned with the dossier's unplaced elements offered; group(name, quarters) creates a named region label group over §9.1's quarter tree nodes. | sugar.json (§7.6); §4.5; §4.7 S10b | fold; §7.5 upgrade; V10 clerk |  |
| DETERMINISM-0352 | mechanism | §7.1 Points in, shape out, handles always (item 101 (i); J-A101.73) | determinism | drawBoundary, drawWater, drawWall, drawWay and placeAt take a points form; the dry run derives the shape with the operator that builds that class and stores it resolved in the event's geometry fields (no replay or later derivation re-derives or moves an accepted shape): a quarter grows from its seed along block edges to its kind's size band by §4.5's grower (two to four points give its far edges; a lasso its outline), snapped to street centre-lines, water, walls and ridges, never through a block, the kind steering the snap (harbour hugs the shore, manor prefers high dry ground, market centres on a square); a lake fills S1's basin to the declared level; a river runs source to mouth by S1's water operator; a coast and harbour take a sea level and one shore point with a basin size, quays by §4.8's harbour rules; a wall follows ridges and contours between points by S5a's trace, closing when ends meet; a road joins two points by S4's router with its class (§4.3); a place takes the plot its point selects (the plot under it, else the nearest lawful one by §4.7 S10b) and S11's site plan. The derived shape keeps four to eight handles by default (more on demand); dragging one is a new declaration of the same kind on the same entity (drawBoundary with the district's id, moveVertex, moveCourseKnot); the authoring surface's feature snap (§9.5) is visible and escapable and resolved into the event's points before the dry run; the kernel's attraction (§3.6) applies to every insertion and is never escaped; a brush is many seeds and a lasso many points: one event kind per thing. | §4.5 grower; §4.2 S1; §4.3; §4.4 S5a; §4.7 S10b; §4.8 harbour; §9.5 snap | V09 canvas handles; V10 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0353 | number | §7.1 | determinism | Derived shapes keep four to eight handles by default. |  | DETERMINISM-0352 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0354 | rule | §7.1 Three surfaces, one vocabulary | determinism (surfaces: V09 application, V10 clerk, §11.1 skeleton) | The canvas (§14's reference UI), the clerk's tool API (over §11.2's embedding API) and the skeleton file (§11.1 — ordered declarations imported as the log's creation prefix and read ahead by their stages, §4.1, §7.7) produce identical events; a clerk's proposal is a dry run shown as a ghost; accepted, it commits with proposedBy 'clerk' and the user as author (J-A101.74); the clerk never bypasses the dry run or the undo, never writes a dossier fact, and reads only what its viewer may know (§9.2); the tool API and these events are the base's; the built-in clerk and plots on a bridge deck are staged (Staged by the owner's roadmap). | §11.1, §11.2; §9.2 knowledge | V09, V10; test DETERMINISM-0237 skeleton round trip |  |
| DETERMINISM-0356 | rule | §7.2 Protection | determinism | Protection: a locked entity (or locked attribute paths) is a fixed input — regeneration never changes it and re-derives dependants around it; a pinned entity keeps its existence and position, other attributes may re-derive; an edited attribute implicitly pins that attribute and the entity's existence against Regenerate and New random (IMPROVEMENTS O9) unless the user chooses discard edits in scope, only by confirming the fate list (§7.3 item 4), recorded in the event; an assigned attribute is held at its assignment within the drift bounds (§7.7). | lock/pin events; §7.7 drift | §7.3 fates; F-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0357 | rule | §7.2 Protection | determinism (§7.7) | A dossier-backed element (existence 'dossier') has its existence held on every map: a map delete is recorded as rejectSite (§7.7); a map event that would close, remove or re-designate it is refused as F-EDIT (§7.7 item 2's list); a regeneration under it re-places it by §4.7 S10b's solver when its plot goes; its position is not held unless the assignment is pinned or a positive placement pins it; a positive placement (placeAt) pins its element or designation to its plot and is kept by every re-derivation and dossier re-application that can honour it, reported when one cannot. | §7.7; §4.7 S10b | §5.3 dossier row (DETERMINISM-0061); F-EDIT | restated in §5.3 row and §7.7 |
| DETERMINISM-0358 | rule | §7.2 Protection | determinism | Every finer face under a locked or pinned entity is a fixed input too; under a warp or smoothing step the kernel holds its route vertices as §3.6 states, so "keeps its position" holds for plots, blocks and buildings (which carry no vertex roles); the dry run lists the held entities; when protections and inputs meet, §7.7's precedence order decides. | §3.6 held vertices; §7.7 precedence | warp; §7.3 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0359 | rule | §7.2 Declared means held (item 101 R13; J-A101.71) | determinism | Every entity an authoring declaration made, placed or shaped is held without a lock: the declaring event pins it (existence and position; other attributes may re-derive) — a drawn feature is locked besides, a placed or assigned thing held as placeAt and assignments hold it, a quarter's boundary and a points form's derived shape pinned by their event, a hand-placed light by placeLight — so Regenerate, New random, every scoped re-roll, an epoch and every later derivation keep it (§5.3's declared row), the regenerate brush skips it, the fates list names it kept (declared) (§7.3 item 4); its author changes it by a new declaration (handle drag), by unpin, or by undoing the declaring event; a skeleton's declarations are held the same way (§4.1); the pieces a closure edit created (§7.3 item 9) are held while the creating edit is live. | declaration events | §5.3 declared row (DETERMINISM-0069); §7.3 item 4 fates |  |
| DETERMINISM-0360 | rule | §7.2 Drawn constraints (G-P8) | determinism | Drawn constraints (ways, water, walls, greens, district boundaries, footprints) enter the plat through insertChain as mandatory chains with their roles (§3.6) after §7.1's resampling; locked by default; apply at their log position: inserted into the current state and, for the coarsest faces its chain touches, its class's local operator (§7.3) re-derives the affected scope — drawn water via S1's water operator (re-sites crossings on moved reaches, re-routes ways using them), drawn ways via S4 and S5 (arterials re-route to reuse them), drawn walls via S7's run re-trace, drawn boundaries via S6's regrouping, drawn greens via S12 on covered faces, drawn footprints via S11 on the host plot; session and replay run the same code at the same log position in the same canonical insertion order (same digest, §6.5). | §3.6 insertChain; §7.3 operators | §6.5 replay = session |  |
| DETERMINISM-0361 | rule | §7.2 Rebuild around my drawings | determinism | The old rule "insert before the stage that would create the class" survives only as the explicit action Rebuild around my drawings: it moves the drawn constraints into the recipe as constraints of their stages, regenerates the town, shows the diff as a migration does (§7.5) and commits as a rebase (reason drawnConstraintsRebuilt) (J-DET-6). | Recipe constraints; §7.5 diff | rebase event (RebaseReason); V09 UI |  |
| DETERMINISM-0362 | mechanism | §7.2 Field brushes | determinism (field model §4.5) | Field brushes: a stroke is a relative Δ over the driver field (FieldStroke); the field is the driver field plus the stroke list composited in log order and clamped once at the end, so undo removes a stroke and re-composites exactly (a per-stroke clamp is not exact: +0.3 then −0.3 on 0.9 would give 0.7); a stroke re-derives every block whose plot samples moved — against the samples their DerivationRecord holds (§2.8) — by more than τ = 0.01 of the field's range, same τ on replay; bands move only as §4.5 states (a stroke in one quarter never re-bands another); an epoch keeps the stroke list and re-composites it over its new drivers; a brush is authorial: the blocks it re-derives take the new field as their built state (S10 and S11 read it; plots' builtBand set to the band they are re-cut for), so the static base behaves as before item 31, whose geometry floor governs wealth change over epochs only (§4.11; J-A31.72); an epoch never re-derives a block by τ — it acts on survivors only through §4.11's ladder bounded by the floor. | §4.5 field; §2.8 DerivationRecord | setField; §7.3 field rows; V01; V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0363 | number | §7.2 | determinism | Field re-derivation threshold τ = 0.01 of the field's range. |  | DETERMINISM-0362 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0364 | mechanism | §7.2 terrain brush | determinism | Terrain brush: edits the heightfield raster under S1's terrain operator (§7.3 (a)) with the same stroke list and single clamp (to the declared relief limit, §2.3); water mode locked (default): stroke masked (Δ zero) on every cell whose centre lies in a water face or within one cell of a shore or bank edge, and under footprints, bands and decks of locked or pinned entities; land cells near the sea keep §4.2 S1 (2)'s floor (a lowering stroke cannot sink the coast); water bodies, courses and widths unchanged; dry run shows masked cells; follow: stroke may change bed and bank cells; every reach whose bed cells changed is re-traced by S1's water operator (§7.3 (b)), re-siting crossings and re-routing ways; in both modes the terrain operator re-bases every building, wing, tower, gate, wall vertex, bridge and pier whose terrain samples changed, re-runs drainage locally, re-evaluates terrain-reading field drivers (authorial like a brush) so the town stands as a reload would compute (J-DET-24), and re-derives the basement decision of every building whose terrain gate changed (§2.6, item 29). | §2.3 relief limit; §4.2 S1 (2) | §7.3 (a); F-SITEPLAN |  |
| DETERMINISM-0365 | rule | §7.3 item 1 Effective scope | determinism | Effective scope: regenerate(scope) finds the coarsest faces covering the scope at its level; that set (possibly larger than the drawn area) is the effective scope, shown before commit and stored in the event as the resolved EditScope; for a chained edit, the union of every chained operator's scope; for a designation change, dossier event, positive placement or relocation (item 8), the plots the placement and mutation touched (the site, its joined neighbour if any, each displaced use's new site) bounded by the request kind's disruption budget (§7.7), except a dossier event over budget, whose scope is its least-disruptive placement's touched set, reported over budget (§7.7 item 5). | EditScope; §7.7 budgets | regenerate; fates |  |
| DETERMINISM-0366 | rule | §7.3 item 1 | determinism | Stored compositions, spanned passages and re-homed households (§2.6): the edit storing a composition (and every event whose re-cut of a stored plan establishes J-P48 (1) for its new walls) holds the faces sharing an edge J-P48 (1) splits and the row neighbours' wing rings its carry changes; an edit whose batches change a Passage face (L3) or move one of its vertices holds the passage's front building when its stored composition has a wing spanning the passage; an event changing a spanning wing's heights or making its building a footprint trace or tombstone holds the passage it spans, rewriting headroomCm or frontBuilding; an edit lowering a building's capacity below its households holds that building, the buildings §2.6's capacity rule re-homes into and, where §4.7 re-provides a dossier household's housing, the plots its dossier request touches, bounded as a dossier event's scope is. | §2.5 Passage; §2.6 carry trigger, capacity rule | V02, V03 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0367 | rule | §7.3 item 1 seam exception (NF-P6) | determinism | Seam exception: a district-scoped re-derivation's effective scope also includes its bounding ways' chains and Corridor records, the half-corridor strips on both sides and the junction wedges at the vertices its batches insert or remove on those spines — on a neighbour's side only II.1 (3)'s neighbour step, one corridor_split edge and the one kerb vertex it lands on (promoted) or adds —, the neighbouring cells, plots and footprints whose rings pass through those vertices and the wing rings of their buildings (§2.6), all changed only by those vertices (a promoted vertex returns to its level when the lane goes, §2.2 rule 3), and the Seam records it is party to (§4.6, II.4). | II.1 (3); §2.2 rule 3; Seam records | V01 neighbourhoods |  |
| DETERMINISM-0368 | rule | §7.3 item 2 Fine levels | determinism | Tree scoping (L1 cells and below): every unprotected finer entity under the effective scope is discarded and the refinement stages re-run for those faces — recorded inputs and unchanged rerolls for Regenerate (keep settings), advanced rerolls for New random; the batch's rederivesFrom and kept finer content follow §3.3; sound by over-invalidation; the primary mechanism. | §3.3 rederivesFrom | regenerate; F-REDERIVE |  |
| DETERMINISM-0369 | rule | §7.3 item 2 Shared-edge parts | determinism | The L3 parts S10's step (0) builds on both sides of an L1 edge two face sets share without corridors (§4.7) are cleared by a re-derivation from S8, S9 or S10 inside its scope; a scope holding wall-strip faces, or faces bordering the settlement perimeter or a reservation boundary two districts share, re-runs step (0) for its own side's parts after its S8 and S9 (first when it re-derives from S10), before its plots: bandUnion restricted to the scope's faces, cross edges ending on the shared edge by attraction onto the other side's vertices, and the scope's plot, alley and furlong lines ending on the re-laid band's inner edge; the other side is never written; so a Regenerate (keep settings) of a perimeter district lays the same backland part, and of the hinterland (S12 row) the same headland, byte-identical to the one-click result. Measurement (M2a, B-EDIT): Regenerate (keep settings) of a perimeter district, a walled district and the hinterland on B-CITY-lite seeds is byte-identical; pre-decided fallback: cover both sides of every shared stretch the scope borders (other side's parts and fronting blocks re-derive from S10 with the scope, declared in its effective scope) (J-DET-29). | §4.7 S10 step (0); bandUnion | §16 M2a; NF-P5 (DETERMINISM-0083) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0370 | rule | §7.3 item 2 | determinism | S13a re-derives label candidates inside the scope and draws names only in §6.3's draw cases; the display list, tiles and label placement update as §8.1 item 4 and §9.2 state. | §6.3 draw cases | §8.1; §9.2 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0371 | rule | §7.3 item 2 Replay mode | determinism | Replay mode (load verification, migration, undo beyond the scoped records, the undo of an upgrade after a reload) folds the log through exactly the session's code path: every model stage, S13a's scoped delta included, runs at its event's log position (name uniqueness resolved scope by scope in log order, §9.6; moveLabel, hideLabel and setAttr(name) address LabelEntity records and names S13a creates mid-log); only the view stages wait: S14 and S13b run once at the end (J-DET-21). | §9.6 | §2.9 load; §7.5; §7.4 undo |  |
| DETERMINISM-0372 | rule | §7.3 item 3 Coarse stages | determinism | L1 and L0 are not per-face refinements (S4 routes across the frame, S5 grows a town-wide queue, S6 grows districts across cells, S7 traces an enceinte), so each has its own local operator with pinned boundaries; an operator may chain others (S1's water operator chains S4, S5, S6, S7): the chain's batches commit in the order stated, each operator draws from the operated entity's lineage under its own stage's concerns, and the whole chain runs in the dry run first (§7.1), so session and replay run the same chain at the same log position; an edit with no local operator (a parameter consumed early, §7.1) is Re-derive from stage k. | §6.1 concerns | operator table (DETERMINISM-0373 ff) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0373 | table-row | §7.3 operator table: S1 terrain | determinism | S1 terrain (terrain brush in either mode, site features, a marsh's reclaim, item 25): operator (a) — masked stroke, local drainage, terrain-reading field drivers, exact re-basing of what stands on the changed ground, slope re-sampling; in follow mode (b) for every reach whose bed changed; pinned: all water in locked mode, the heightfield outside the stroke, every way's geometry; beyond: in follow mode Re-derive from S1 when a course leaves its drainage basin or F-RIVER needs its inlet or centre rung (§5.2). | (a) text | terrain brush; convert reclaim |  |
| DETERMINISM-0374 | table-row | §7.3 operator table: S1 water | determinism | S1 water (drawWater, a moved shore or coast vertex, moveCourseKnot, a warp from level 0, the terrain brush in follow mode, siltReach, shiftCourse and a foreshore's reclaim): operator (b) in §4.11's order — new water, crossings, routes, wall runs and the chain's footprint as data; one L1 batch clearing the footprint's partition content, re-laying kept ways through their decks, re-tracing crossed wall runs and cutting lesser ways; one L0 batch inserting the water through the vertices that batch prepared, both declaring the footprint as clear region; then S5, S6, S7, tree scoping and F-REDERIVE; pinned: reaches and coasts outside the edit, every way, wall and district not named in (b), every finer face outside the footprint less the seam exception at its boundary; beyond: Re-derive from S1 when the course leaves its drainage basin or F-RIVER needs its inlet or centre rung. | (b) text; §4.11 | water edits |  |
| DETERMINISM-0375 | table-row | §7.3 operator table: S4 | determinism | S4 (drawWay of an arterial, a moved arterial vertex, a moved destination, the castle placed or resized, a deck re-sited or removed by S1's water operator): re-route only the arterials whose corridors meet the edit region or whose destination or deck moved, each between its nearest pinned nodes (junctions of degree ≥ 3 or destination nodes) on either side of the region, over S3's lattice, every deck a fixed graph edge; pinned: their endpoints and every spine outside the region; beyond: a gate-count change (a destination added or removed) re-derives from the stage picking those gates — S3 for stage 1 (§4.2 S3 (7)), S5 of its stage for a later enceinte (§4.4). | §4.3 router | F-ROUTE |  |
| DETERMINISM-0376 | table-row | §7.3 operator table: S5 | determinism | S5 (drawWay of a secondary, a deleted street, a secondary now crossing water off a deck): regrow only inside the affected L1 cells from the proposals whose origins lie there, under S5c's legality (water only on a deck); pinned: the cells' boundary spines; beyond: none. | §4.4 S5c | drawWay |  |
| DETERMINISM-0377 | table-row | §7.3 operator table: S6 | determinism | S6 (drawBoundary, district merge or split, outskirtToDistrict, land vacated or covered by a moved channel): regroup and re-type only the districts touching the region, the grower restricted to their cells, turn skips and picks from district.seed keyed by each grower's seed lineage and turn (§4.5, §6.1; item 47), so unchanged cells regroup as before; then re-lay the margin bands: along every L1 edge without corridors whose sharing changed (moved perimeter stretch, a reservation boundary two districts now share) S10's step (0) lays the 2 m band on both sides, and where a merge or conversion ends the sharing it removes both parts; blocks fronting such a stretch on either side (neighbour's or hinterland's included) re-derive from S10 and join the effective scope shown in the dry run; pinned: every other district and every block fronting no changed stretch. | §4.5 grower; S10 step (0) | drawBoundary etc.; V01 |  |

| DETERMINISM-0391 | mechanism | §7.3 (a) 3-6 | determinism | (a) 3 Fields — the terrain-reading drivers (elevation above the flood line, low and damp ground, §4.5) re-evaluated on the dilated box; blocks whose plot samples moved beyond τ re-derive as for a field brush (§7.2), matching a reload (§2.9); inside an epoch event (a reclaim the engine schedules, §4.11) the re-evaluated drivers only mark due blocks for §4.11's ladder and re-derive nothing by τ. 4 Re-basing, exact — every entity on a changed sample re-based by §2.6's per-entity datum step (the function S11 and S7 call): a wing's baseCm = max(samples) + plinthCm with its foundation skirt; a row footprint whose fall now exceeds skirtMaxCm re-split into stepped wings (F-SITEPLAN's first rung; footprint face and address unchanged); towers and gates from samples under their footprints; the wall's base profile (each knot whose sample changed takes the new value; a trace vertex whose sample changed and carries no knot gains one, §2.2 rule 5); a bridge's deckCm and a pier's deck height from bank elevations plus the clearance row; a user-edited baseCm kept (fate kept, listed; J-DET-24); every building whose terrain gate changed re-derives its basement decision by §2.6 (a cellar under a raised water table becomes none, a slope past the class threshold a walk-out cellar), an edited basement kept (item 29). 5 Slopes — every way whose spine crosses changed cells re-samples slopeP95; a way now steeper than its class's limit is reported with an offer to re-route it (a regenerate through S4's or S5's operator), never re-routed silently. 6 Water — in follow mode, (b) runs for every reach whose bed cells changed. | §4.5 drivers; §2.6 datum step, basement rules | F-SITEPLAN; V02 basements |  |
| DETERMINISM-0392 | mechanism | §7.3 (b) preamble | determinism | (b) S1's water operator runs in §4.11's carve order — prepare the finer structure the new water will meet, insert the water, re-grow what it displaced — because a coarse insertion may meet finer geometry only at vertices an earlier batch of the same edit placed (§3.3); both geometry batches declare the chain's footprint as their clear region under §3.3's clearing and preparation rules (as the carve's batches (1)-(2) do); §4.11's terrain and water conversions (item 25: silting, course shift, foreshore reclamation) run through this operator as local repair, their new water or land being (1)'s data; a marsh's reclamation runs (a), digCanal the carve. | §3.3 clear region rules; §4.11 carve order | water edits; V06 conversions |  |
| DETERMINISM-0393 | step | §7.3 (b) 1 | determinism | (b) 1 New water as data (nothing committed), by trigger: moveCourseKnot — the course between the moved knot's neighbours recomputed through the knots by §4.2 S1 (3)'s control-polyline construction with the moved knot fixed (an edited knot is implicitly pinned, §7.2), width and depth from accumulation, shores = course buffered by width; a carve beyond carveMaxCm returns F-EDIT with the nearest position along the drag within it. A follow terrain stroke — each reach whose bed cells changed re-traced by descent from the nearest pinned upstream point to where the new course first meets the old (or its outfall), F-RIVER's re-trace rungs confined to the reach; knots re-sampled at the control spacing with fresh ordinals, except an edited knot the new course must pass within half a control spacing of, else F-EDIT offering to discard that edit. drawWater — the drawn rings (add unioned, subtract removed; a line buffered by its width), a drawn river's or canal's course being its line. A moved shore or coast vertex, or a level-0 warp — the shores as the move leaves them (the stroke's field applied to the reach's L0 shore and coast vertices, the rest of the brush moving land at level 1 by §3.6's warp first), the course recomputed as their centreline, knots re-sampled; nothing re-traced (the user's shore authoritative, §2.3). The changed reach is where new shores differ from old; an edited coast stretch has its sea side re-lowered and contours clipped (F-CONTOUR); new shore segments take bank class and kind by §4.2 S1 (7). | §4.2 S1 (3), (7); carveMaxCm | F-RIVER; F-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0394 | step | §7.3 (b) 2 | determinism | (b) 2 Crossings, routes, wall runs and footprint as data: siteCrossings (§4.2 S1 (5)) on the changed reach (sites perpendicular to new flow, ends on new banks); each old deck there (built bridge or unused site) matched to its successor site (new site nearest its old midpoint within the search band, §6.3's bridge row); kept ways (F-REDERIVE) re-routed by S4's router over S3's lattice between their nearest pinned nodes — new channel ⊕ attractCm impassable except across a deck, every site a candidate deck and fixed graph edge, the way's own successor site preferred within the detour bound; F-ROUTE's rungs decide (an extra-bridge deck sited now); a deck built at every site a route uses; the one at an old deck's successor site takes the old bridge's address, name and assignments when its way uses it; every other new site stays a site (two bank vertices, no span) that (4) inserts with the shores; a way whose old deck lay on the changed reach but which the new water no longer meets keeps its geometry and (9) turns that deck into an ordinary segment; every enceinte run the new or old course crosses re-traced between its nearest unaffected towers, a water gate where the new course crosses and none at the old; the footprint computed last: with B = (new channel ∪ changed old channel) ⊕ (attractCm + bank setback), the exact union of the L1 cells that (i) B meets (vacated land and cells S6 re-assigns); (ii) border a stretch (3) cuts, prunes or replaces, or that a kept way's new route crosses; (iii) border the old run of a re-traced wall or a stretch of its setback, glacis or intervallum line (3) removes with it (between the feet of the perpendiculars from the run's end towers), or meet the re-traced run ⊕ (half its band + setback, + glacis or berm on the field side); these are the carve's footprint rules (§4.11); the fate report lists exactly the protected entities inside it. | §4.2 S1 (5) siteCrossings; §4.3; §6.3 bridge row | F-ROUTE; F-REDERIVE |  |

| DETERMINISM-0400 | rule | §7.3 item 4 | determinism | A replay or receiving client compares an event's stored fates and renames with those it computes only when it folds the event under the output version that confirmed them — the from of the first live upgrade after the event, else the document's own output version — and reports a mismatch when they differ; folded under a later version the recomputed values are applied: the upgrade names those events once in its diff (MigrationMap.changedConfirmations, §7.5) and no later load re-reports them; the upgrade's undo, replaying under from, compares them again. | §7.5 MigrationMap | §2.9 load; §11.3 bridge clients |  |

| DETERMINISM-0407 | test | §7.3 item 7 (v) | determinism | (v) Coarse edits: terrain-brush (both modes), course-knot, drawn-water, moved-shore, level-0 warp, drawn-way and drawn-wall sequences end with 0 hard invariant violations, 0 decks and 0 sites over land, 0 off-deck crossings, 0 wings off §2.6's datum rule, 0 footprints in water, an event for every demolition, cut and re-route, every arterial at its destinations or reported unserved, every bridge that still has a site within the band keeping its address and name; a river move across a street grid commits (no E_LEVEL or E_KEPT) with decks on kept ways and cuts on others, every face outside (b)'s footprint byte-identical; a pond drawn inside a block and a river bank widened into its riverside blocks between two bridges each commit with no E_LEVEL or E_KEPT and byte identity outside; a river moved across a wall between two towers commits likewise (every indexed role unique per ordinal, Gate.banks on its two gate_end vertices, no anchor contact name left, 0 NF-P5 violations); a river moved across the settlement perimeter leaves 0 district faces outside it; a course-knot move replayed after an upgrade that changed S1's knot count binds by address and ordinal or is reported orphaned, never another body's knot. | DETERMINISM-0389-DETERMINISM-0397 | F-REDERIVE test (DETERMINISM-0052) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0408 | test | §7.3 item 7 (vi)-(vii) | determinism | (vi) Shared-edge identity: Regenerate (keep settings) of a perimeter district, a walled district and the hinterland byte-identical to the one-click result; after outskirtToDistrict, drawBoundary and a district merge every L1 edge two face sets share without corridors carries a margin band on both sides and no other edge carries one. (vii) Mutations and dossier events: a designation change, relocation, plot join or split, dossier event, positive placement, rejection, a relet with the stored-plan re-cut it runs, a plan edit or rung re-roll, a closure edit with its undo and the deletion of its edited thing, and a sugar event with its expansion are edits under (i)-(iii): byte identity outside their touched set, the same record on every host, and A → B → A by the inverse mutation byte-identical when nothing else changed (§4.7 S11 rule 5; fixtures §13.2's). | DETERMINISM-0369; DETERMINISM-0377; §4.7 S11 rule 5 | §13.2 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0409 | rule | §7.3 item 8 (item 18; J-A18.71) | determinism | Designation changes are mutations, not re-derivations: where an edit changes what a plot is for (assign, unassign, setAttr on a plot's use or class, openUse, closeUse, relocateUse, a dossier event, placeAt, the re-placement a rejectSite causes) the engine does not discard the plot's finer content and re-run S11: it runs §4.7 S11's designation mutator, which keeps every part still fitting the new designation's template, adapts, converts, adds and subtracts the rest as a recorded MutationRecord drawn from the plot's siteplan.mutate stream (§6.1), and joins a neighbour only when the template's size budget needs the land (a join chained as item 3 states); effective scope those plots' faces; fates are item 4's (a locked part kept, never subtracted); reversal applies the inverse record (§7.4); tree scoping remains the path of Regenerate (keep settings) and New random on the same plot. | §4.7 S11 mutator; siteplan.mutate | DETERMINISM-0315; DETERMINISM-0382; §7.4 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0410 | rule | §7.3 item 9 (a) closure edits (item 66; J-A66.71) | determinism | Closure rule (owner: "if a user edits a building to have an access to the underground, it generates all the necessary pieces"): an edit whose target lacks its prerequisites is expanded at its dry run into its closure — the minimal set of pieces making it lawful, in dependency order (each piece's prerequisites first); an edit kind that can imply prerequisites declares its closure generators in the entity registry (§2.8), in order, each naming the stage operator it calls; a kind declaring none is never expanded; the closure is the edit's ONE event: its batches (each one level of one arena, and on the underground layer of one part — §3.3, §3.4, §4.1) run in dependency order on the dry run's overlay and commit together (whole or not at all). | §2.8 entity registry generators | addUndergroundAccess; §5.3 closures row |  |
| DETERMINISM-0411 | rule | §7.3 item 9 (b) | determinism | Closure seed and locality: every piece draws from the edited entity's and its target's lineage streams under the calling stage's concerns keyed with the event's seq (§6.1), minted from the event's seq and its ordinal in dependency order (§6.3; J-A18.43's pattern): one edit at one log position of one seed always yields one set of pieces; effective scope = the pieces and their clear regions; byte identity outside (item 7 (ii)). | §6.1; §6.3 | DETERMINISM-0404 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0412 | rule | §7.3 item 9 (c) | determinism | Closure record, undo, ownership: the event stores EditBase.closure (created pieces in dependency order, and reused existing pieces — a connector already dug, another cellar); undo removes exactly the created pieces and restores what they replaced (§7.4), nothing reused touched; a later delete of the edited thing (the access point) removes the created pieces nothing else now uses (a connector a second access shares stays, named among that access's reused pieces); a basement the closure created returns to §2.6's derived decision unless edited since; pieces held while the edit is live (§7.2); a closure creates map facts only, never a dossier fact (item 20 (h)); a closure that would change a dossier-owned value is refused dossier-owned (§5.2). | ClosureRecord | §7.4 addUndergroundAccess row; §5.3 closures row |  |
| DETERMINISM-0413 | rule | §7.3 item 9 (d) | determinism / V04 underground | Closure instances: addUndergroundAccess(building, target?) is the first; its generators are II.7.6's pieces (J-A66.41): the basement by §2.6's requested-mode ladder (the request overrides the probability, never the terrain gate: a full cellar, else a half cellar or walk-out on a slope, else a hatch to a crawl space, else a refusal naming the gate); the target in II.7.6's order; the dug connector; the minimal private underground where none exists; the access point; the plan reservation (the basement plan's stair, ladder or hatch, its rung marked stale, §2.8); the links. A clandestine front lacking its concealed door (§4.7, item 63; the setAttr setting a placed use's concealment to front, acting on the use's building; T66-5) and an undercity neighbourhood an edit leaves without a lifeline (II.7.5 condition 4, item 65; a drawBoundary adding one on the undercity's part or a split of one, acting on that neighbourhood's District, whose lineage keys the lifeline's draws) are closures too — a concealed door by §2.6's entrance rules (§4.7 Concealed institutions (4)), a lifeline by II.7.6's target, connector, access and link pieces; every later edit kind implying missing prerequisites declares its generators the same way; the closure rule and the edit kind are the base's; addUndergroundAccess's underground pieces run once the underground stages exist (II.7.4; staged), until then its dry run returns F-EDIT naming the staged layer (§5.2). | II.7.6; §2.6 requested-mode ladder; §4.7 concealed (4) | V04; F-EDIT; T66-5 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0414 | rule | §7.3 item 9 (e) | determinism | Closure failure: a generator that cannot produce its piece ends the expansion; the dry run returns F-EDIT with the nearest lawful form its ladder reached or the refusal with its reason (§5.2), and nothing commits. | generators | F-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0415 | interface | §7.3 item 9 | determinism | ClosureRecord { created: { id; piece 'basement'\|'target'\|'connector'\|'system'\|'access'\|'reservation'\|'link'\|'door' }[] (dependency order); reused: Id[] } — EditBase.closure; read by undo, by a later delete and by §5.3's closure row. | closure generators | undo; delete; §5.3 closures row (DETERMINISM-0075) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0417 | rule | §7.3 item 10 (item 101 R5; J-A101.75) | determinism | Scoped re-rolls and variations: every re-roll is scoped (regenerate newRandom on a scope — a block, a quarter, a building's rung — or rerollLayer on one rerollable layer); an unscoped new town is only the empty state's action before anything is declared; a derived fill (a fill, T8's brush, a scoped New random) offers three variations: its dry run derives the scope at its next three distinct rerolls on three overlays (§5.1 (6)'s check advancing a variation equal to the current scope's or an earlier variation's content to the next unused reroll, within reroll.maxAdvance), the ghost shows them side by side; the accepted variation commits as that regenerate with its absolute reroll map, the others leave nothing; declared entities are kept in every variation (§7.2). | DETERMINISM-0017; DETERMINISM-0019 | T8 fill tool; V09/V10 ghost | restates 0314 (unscoped new town) |
| DETERMINISM-0418 | rule | §7.4 Replay semantics | determinism | The log is an ordered fold: each event applies to the state its predecessors produced, through the same code path interactively and on replay; nothing is hoisted; epochs, rebases, upgrades, undo and redo are events like any other. | DETERMINISM-0158 | replay = session |  |
| DETERMINISM-0419 | rule | §7.4 The one read-ahead | determinism | The one read-ahead is the creation prefix: the base derivation's S2 reads the log's creation events (the blueprint's and creation dossier's assign events at positions 0…k) as fixed rows before it runs, and each stage reads a skeleton's drawn declarations there as its constraints (§4.1, item 101; J-A101.41); folding those events afterwards records the generation run's placements and places nothing a second time (§4.2; J-A22.41, J-A22.72, J-A22.74). | EditBase.creation | S2 (§4.2); §4.1 | restates 0158 (§6.2) |
| DETERMINISM-0420 | rule | §7.4 creation events | determinism | A creation event belongs to the base derivation it feeds, not an edit of the map it made: never an undo, redo or revert target — the undo key and revert skip it, and an undo(h, of) naming it returns F-EDIT offering unassign for a blueprint assignment and rejectSite and placeAt for a dossier element (§5.2) — and never truncated; a blueprint assignment is removed by unassign (§7.3 item 8's mutation), a skeleton's declaration by a later declaration or a delete, a dossier element only by a dossier event (§7.7 item 2). | EditBase.creation | F-EDIT; §7.4 undo key |  |
| DETERMINISM-0421 | rule | §7.4 dossier events not undoable | determinism (§7.7) | Every later dossier event (EditBase.dossier set, §7.7 item 1) is no undo, redo or revert target of a map event: revert skips it, an undo(h, of) naming it returns F-EDIT offering rejectSite and placeAt for each dossier element it placed or re-designated (a dossier unassign leaves none to offer); an epoch that carried one in EpochInputs.dossier is refused as an undo target the same way, because undoing either would change dossier content without a dossier instruction (item 20 (c)). | EditBase.dossier; EpochInputs.dossier | F-EDIT; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0423 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | lock / unlock / pin / unpin — create: UI or API on any address, optionally on attribute paths; read: entity flags (derived from the log); persist: log; regenerate: a fixed input, dependants re-derive around it; a lock on a name path keeps the naming record dormant (§2.8); an unlock that frees the name runs case (4); undo: inverse event; undoing a lock that frees a name runs case (4); an unlock that re-drew a name keeps the former name and naming record in its undo record, which its undo restores; migrate: address mapped (§7.5); an orphaned lock re-imposes its last geometry as a drawn constraint unless the user drops it. | DETERMINISM-0252-DETERMINISM-0253 | fold |  |
| DETERMINISM-0424 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | setAttr / setAttrMany / paint — create: UI / API, a bulk query resolved to ids at dry run; read: entity attributes, flags.edited, editedPaths; persist: log; regenerate: an input override at the stage computing the attribute (a name: S13a keeps it and never re-draws while edited, naming record dormant; a district kind: S6 reads it as fixed; heights and roof: S11); implicitly pinned; a plot's tenure and a district's identity are overrides of derived values kept until reverted (items 70, 72); a change of a front's designation runs S11's mutator on the front, the concealed use persisting and its concealed rooms re-fitted (item 63); a setAttr of a dossier-owned value never reaches the fold (dry run refuses); undo: inverse (old values in the undo record); undoing or reverting a setAttr on a generated name returns it to unedited with its dormant naming record and runs case (4), the replaced text kept in that event's undo record; a setAttr of heights or class that re-homed households keeps their former buildings in its undo record; migrate: attribute names schema-versioned with a rename table; unmappable → orphan, reported. | DETERMINISM-0271-DETERMINISM-0272 | fold |  |
| DETERMINISM-0425 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | moveVertex / warp — create: vertex handle stored as lattice point + role signature / staged warp session with Apply; read: geometry; persist: log — the operation (arena key, strokes, fromLevel; a moveVertex's at with its VertexSig); per-vertex displacements only as a verification record; regenerate: the kernel's whole-step validation, halving, sliding and holding (§3.6); kernel-pinned vertices move (pins constrain smoothing, simplification and cutting); what stands on the ground carried and re-based; moved water to S1's water operator; undo: undo record — moved vertices, re-derived faces, former birth levels and flags of promoted and demoted vertices and edges, former points of displaced records, former wing rings, basement rings and stored compositions, former bases of re-based entities, households §2.6's capacity rule re-homed with former buildings; migrate: the operation replays at its log position; a moveVertex binds by resolveVertexAt (exact point, else signature match within the rebind radius, else orphaned and reported). | DETERMINISM-0254, DETERMINISM-0256 | fold |  |

| DETERMINISM-0429 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism / V04 underground | drawTunnel / drawChamber / addLink / placeAccess (items 11, 12, 64, 65; J-A12.71) — create: the underground tools (II.7; staged, editable in the base); read: the Tunnel or Gallery record and its chain, the Chamber record and outline, the UndergroundLink record and its two link_end vertices (each in its system's part), the AccessPoint record, its state, knownTo and access_point vertex; persist: log (geometry payload); regenerate: at its log position — a drawn tunnel through II.7's network stage (chain on the underground arena, navigable derived) and the underground stage re-deriving the blocks its edges bound; a placed access point by II.7.3's access rules, its ground host found by pointLocate, nothing on the ground written; a gallery or chamber through its system's stage on its own part, a link between two parts' vertices one part per batch (§3.4); an access point's underground end by moveVertex and its opening by setAttr on ground.at (refused as F-EDIT outside admitted ground); undo: the replaced underground scope, a moved opening's former ground.at and host; migrate: the same geometry replays; an access point whose element is unmapped is orphaned and reported. | DETERMINISM-0280-DETERMINISM-0284 | fold; V04 |  |

| DETERMINISM-0433 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | placeLight / setAttr / delete on a light (item 68) — create: the light tool (§7.1); read: the LightSource record (§2.7); persist: log; regenerate: a hand-placed light is declared (§7.2): survives a regeneration by its anchor (host's address, rule row, ordinal, §6.3) and follows its host's lifecycle (§2.8: removed with a demolished host, unlit on an abandoned one); rule-placed lights re-derive with their host's faces (§4.7, §4.11); undo: inverse, exact; migrate: by its host's address; a light whose host is unmapped is orphaned and reported. | DETERMINISM-0302 | fold | DANGLING-REFERENCE: "the light tool (§7.1)" — §7.1 defines no light tool |

use kinds; sealing kinds; terrain and water kinds of §4.11; the underground layer's flood, raiseGrade, clearUndercity, II.7.5-II.7.6) — create: context actions; the evolution engine's plan (§4.11); read: lifecycle events with cause; persist: log; regenerate: structural ops on the plat (§3.4): a district or way split/merge with dependants re-derived; a plot join or split is item 31's fusion or splitting (S10 (7)'s re-cut with neighbours fixed, S11's mutator, no occupied building removed unless confirmed); convert = retypeEdges plus lifecycle events; decline conversions change existence and the drawing (§2.8), credit or debit the material ledger (§4.11), a reuse … build succeeds at the trace's address; tunnel conversions change existence and closure (condition reads them, §2.7; J-A11.21) and close or reopen the frontage plots on the run (II.7.3); island conversions resize an arena or archive it (fallIsland lays the ruin face through the carve path); use conversions open, close or relocate a Use with detail.from/to and mutate buildings by S11; relet sets a building's household count within §2.6's capacity (item 28 (f)) — households added minted from the event, leaving households go by item 17's relocation — marks an unstored plan stale and re-cuts a stored one inside the event, no shell geometry changed; densify/thin … infill add or clear yard buildings by S11's mutator; densify/thin … back_court re-cut the plot's yard by S10 (7) with neighbours fixed, adding or removing a rear row and its passage face (§4.6, §4.11); subdivide and amalgamate are rung 3's re-cuts, whose hand forms are §7.1's plot split and join; sealPassage/openPassage set Passage.sealed, change no geometry, vacate or reoccupy the plots behind it (item 30); siltReach, shiftCourse and a foreshore's reclaim run S1's water operator, a marsh's reclaim its terrain operator, digCanal the carve; a split or join of a chain carrying a profile (a way's width, a wall's base, a tunnel's invert, a shore's bank kind or class) goes through retypeEdges' rekey on the part's StretchRef (§3.4) — each part gets a cut knot carrying the sampled value, a join knot dropped only where the joined profile is unchanged (§2.2 rule 5) — and the event writes both records' returned profiles; undo: members resurrect with former rings, records and builtBand, tombstones return to live, both records' former profiles; a decline or tunnel conversion's former state and ledger rows; a fallen island's arena restored from the archive and its ruin face removed through the carve path's inverse; a reuse … build undo returning the address to the trace; a use conversion's former use and building links; a relet's former count, household links and stored plan; a ladder conversion's former rings, records and passage face; a terrain or water conversion's replaced chain scope with §2.3's records; a mutation's inverse record; migrate: same. | DETERMINISM-0275, DETERMINISM-0300; §4.11 ConversionOp | fold; V04; V05; V06 | MIGRATE-CELL: "same" (ambiguous — same as which row?) |
