| DETERMINISM-0454 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism / V01 neighbourhoods | closeup (stage 2) — create: logged before the first edit inside a close-up; read: the close-up's entities, a refinement level of the district; persist: log (strategy, params) and the edits after it; regenerate: a pure function of the district's committed state, sub-seed, strategy and params; a base re-derivation of the district re-derives its close-up with carry-over, re-matches presentation instance edits by position and reports orphans; undo: undo record; migrate: replays. | DETERMINISM-0303 | fold; V01 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0455 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism / V06 evolution | epoch — create: grow(doc, inputs) (§4.11) or a circumstance-schedule entry; read: the derived EpochRecord, lifecycle events with cause; persist: log; regenerate: §4.11's incremental path at its log position — each plot's fieldBand re-banded after S6 (§2.5), regrowth-first siting, the survivors' ladder bounded by the geometry floor (its rung-1 relet marking unstored plans stale and re-cutting stored ones inside the epoch, item 28 (f)), the use step, the decline lottery, the island and underground laws; every live rejection re-checked by §7.7's lapse rule (item 3); a dossier change the host sends with the epoch rides in EpochInputs.dossier and is applied at §4.11's step (e), one sent between epochs folding at its own log position (§7.7 item 6; item 22 (e)); the epoch carries its absolute week (item 76), the Works it starts are records whose stages the week derives (item 67), its character step recomputes each district's vector, moves identities past their hysteresis, writes the pressures and renames by §6.3's case (5) (§4.5, item 72); undo: the faces and entities it replaced (O(epoch scope)) with the identities, pressures and names it wrote; migrate: a multi-epoch document upgrades by replaying the whole ordered log with a per-epoch diff, or stays pinned to its output version. | DETERMINISM-0304; §4.11 | fold; V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0456 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism / model §2.9 | rebase — create: keeping the saved town on a mismatch; Rebuild around my drawings; editing a frozen import (a frozenImport rebase runs case (4) over every unedited generated name and returns confirm with renames listed; keeping the old model at an upgrade needs none — the document stays at its own output version, §7.5); read: the document's base; persist: the base state and the event (version names the output version that computed stateDigest; at the log head the state materialises — h₀ when kept after edits during load verification, §2.9 step 4; events before at stay in the log, §2.9); regenerate: replay starts from the rebased state, checked against its root of record for the replay's output version (§2.9 step 3), and folds the events after at; other clients: see Rebases on other clients; undo: the previous base; migrate: the rebased state migrates by the schema chain; an upgrade checks it under from against its root of record and records its root under to as baseDigest (§7.5); not seed-reproducible. | DETERMINISM-0305 | fold; §11 bridge |  |
| DETERMINISM-0457 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | upgrade — create: explicit, after the diff (§7.5); read: the document's output version; persist: log with baseDigest for a rebased base and the saved packs in its mapping; regenerate: §7.5 — a rebased base checked under from and re-rooted under to; every pack to stops shipping copied into packs first; the mapping hands names on with addresses and runs case (4) for unedited names whose naming slices the new tables changed, listed in the diff; earlier events' fates and renames recomputed under to and differences listed once in the diff; undo: the undo record is upgrade.from — undo replays base and log before the upgrade under that version (a rebased base checked against its root of record for from) to exactly the pre-upgrade digest; in session the pre-upgrade checkpoint makes it instant; outside the browser's version window the undo is withdrawn there with the CLI command shown; migrate: —. | DETERMINISM-0306; §7.5 | fold |  |
| DETERMINISM-0458 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | undo / redo — create: ctrl / cmd+Z, ctrl / cmd+shift+Z, the API's undo(h) / redo(h), a host's doc.apply; read: each event's live or undone status, derived from the log; persist: log (of); regenerate: after the independence check — undo restores its target's scope from the target's undo record; redo re-applies the target at the head, its lifecycle events citing the target's seq and its minted addresses the target's own; a result freeing a name runs case (4); undo: an undo is undone by a redo, a redo by an undo (exact inverses); migrate: replays; a target's record is rebuilt under the new version by the replay. | DETERMINISM-0307 | fold; §14 API |  |
| DETERMINISM-0459 | lifecycle-row | §7.4 table | determinism | host provider output — create: a host-registered provider's answer after the double-run check (§6.5 (8)); read: the document's hostContent block; persist: hostContent keyed by (provider id, version, input hash); regenerate: read from hostContent on replay, never re-requested; undo: removed with the event that first requested it; migrate: replays without the host. | DETERMINISM-0230 | §2.9 hostContent |  |
| DETERMINISM-0460 | rule | §7.4 Rebases | determinism | A rebase event is a no-op for a client whose base it already is; a replay from the recipe folds an undone rebase and its undo as a no-op pair, every event between them undone (a whole-town event is undoable only as the latest live event); undo: when the previous base is the recipe (always kept in a rebased base) the undo restores it and replays the kept log; when it is an earlier rebased state, that state is the undo record held in the undo section under its cap; past the cap, or when the replay would need an output version outside the browser's window, the undo is withdrawn with a notice (the CLI replays any version, §6.4); a redo re-installs the rebased state, which the undo keeps under the same cap (past it the redo is withdrawn with a notice). | DETERMINISM-0305; undo section cap (DETERMINISM-0467) | DETERMINISM-0456 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0461 | rule | §7.4 Rebases on other clients | determinism / §11.3 bridge | Rebases on other clients of a bridged document: a keptSnapshot rebase whose at lies behind the client's head and whose stateDigest differs from the client's root at at installs the snapshot at at from the doc.load bytes the client kept and re-applies the events after at through the fold (fates recomputed, differences reported, undo records rebuilt; §2.9 step 4); one whose stateDigest equals that root changes nothing; any other rebase behind the head (a hand-built log) is a reported no-op; a load whose log already holds a keptSnapshot rebase at model.seq with stateDigest = model.digest takes model as that base without asking. | §2.9 step 4; §11.3 doc.load | §11.3 bridge |  |
| DETERMINISM-0462 | rule | §7.4 Undo and redo are events | determinism | Undo never removes an event from a shared log (a host stores it and it only grows, §11.3): { t: 'undo'; of } and { t: 'redo'; of } are appended at the head and applied through the same fold, identically by every client and every replay (J-DET-16). | DETERMINISM-0307 | §11.3 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0463 | rule | §7.4 Sessions | determinism / §14 @plat/api | Sessions: an authoring identity per document and author, not per app start; @plat/api mints one (128-bit random value in base 32, outside the model packages) the first time an author edits a document and reuses it on every later open: an unshared document stores it in view state (view.keys['session'], outside every digest, saved with the file, §2.9), so a reload or reopening continues the same undo and redo stacks; a file made without one (CLI or link) mints it on first edit; a client attached to a host bridge takes the session the host assigns in doc.load (§11.3) and never writes the view key; the session enters the log only as EditBase.session; the fold never reads it (an undo names its target by of), so sessions decide which event the undo key picks, never the model (J-DET-34). | §2.9 view keys; §11.3 doc.load | EditBase.session; undo key |  |
| DETERMINISM-0464 | rule | §7.4 Targets | determinism | Each session has its own stacks; undo targets the session's latest live event other than a creation event, a dossier event or an epoch that carried one — live unless an undo targets it and no later redo re-applies it; a group counts as one step and of names its first event; redo targets the session's latest undo whose target is still undone; a new event from the session clears its redo stack; an unshared document has one stored session (linear undo continuing across reloads); a session with the owner role (the app's role for an unshared document) may name any live event (undo(h, of), §11.2), which is how an earlier session's events are undone. | DETERMINISM-0463 | §11.2 undo(h, of) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0465 | mechanism | §7.4 Application | determinism | Undo record: every committed event keeps the inverse event when exact; otherwise the faces, entity records, lifecycle events and tombstones its effective scope replaced, the former birth levels, split flags and stripped contact names of the vertices it promoted, and the former levels and flags of the vertices and edges it demoted or unflagged (§2.2 rule 3; CommitRecord.promoted, demoted, unflagged); and always the addresses it created, changed or removed (its touched set), so undo costs O(scope); undo restores the target's scope from the record and withdraws the addresses the target minted (§6.3); redo re-applies the target at the head from its stored resolved payload (scope, absolute rerolls, confirmed fates), reproducing its result exactly; an edit whose capacity re-homing moves households (§2.6) keeps the former records of the buildings moved out of and into and, where it re-provides a dossier household's housing (§4.7), the plots the re-provision touched (the site with the residential slot, its joined neighbour, each displaced use's new site), restoring them with the households on undo. | §3.3 CommitRecord; §2.2 rule 3 | every lifecycle row's undo column; §2.9 undo section |  |
| DETERMINISM-0467 | rule | §7.4 Independence | determinism | Independence: an undo is applied only if every live event after its target is independent of it — the later event's touched set and effective-scope faces are disjoint from the target's touched set and from the target's effective-scope faces dilated by one ring of adjacent faces at the coarsest level either touches; otherwise the dry run returns F-EDIT listing the dependent events and their authors; the session may undo its own dependent events first, and an owner may undo through a chain — never through a dossier event or an epoch that carried one, so a target one of them depends on stays live; a redo is checked the same way against the live events after its undo; every client folds the same events in the host's order on the same state, so the check agrees everywhere; an undo or redo whose check fails at its log position (only in a hand-built log) is a reported no-op, identically everywhere; a whole-town event (upgrade, rebase, Re-derive from S1) is undoable only while it is the latest live event. | DETERMINISM-0465-DETERMINISM-0466 | F-EDIT; dry run |  |
| DETERMINISM-0468 | rule | §7.4 Independence through the input layer (Fable review 2026-09-27; J-Fdet-1) | determinism | The check also holds a later live event dependent on its target when the target changed an input that event's derivation read: for an editTable or editPreset, when an entity the later event derived (one in its touched set whose leaf digest a stage, S13a's delta, a re-cut or a rung wrote — marked apart from addresses merely set) cites in its derivation record, naming record or provenance (§2.8) a slice whose hash the target's row operations changed (recomputed by §7.6's slice kinds); for an adjust (a parameter, a Re-derive from stage k or the pack list) and a town-wide rerollLayer of naming, whenever the later event derived any entity; an undo or redo whose target is one of these counts as one; an event deriving nothing depends on no such target; so an input change is undone through its dependants or stays, the state after a permitted undo is the fold of the log without its target, and no derivation record cites a slice the effective tables no longer hold; the check costs the later events' touched sets and records' slice references, kept for every event. | §2.8 records; §7.6 slice kinds | undo dry run |  |
| DETERMINISM-0469 | test | §7.4 Independence fixtures | determinism | Fixtures (B-EDIT, §13.2): an editTable on a heights row followed by a build on a plot the edit did not touch, an adjust followed by a drawWay, and a town-wide rerollLayer(naming) followed by a scoped regeneration drawing a name — each later event reported dependent, and once it is undone first the target's undo gives the model and records digests of the log without the pair; an editTable followed by a build whose derivation cites none of the changed slices is independent, and the target's undo gives the digests of the log without the target. | DETERMINISM-0468 | §13.2 B-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0470 | rule | §7.4 Records on other clients and after a reload | determinism | Undo records kept for the session and saved in the file's undo section (the last 64 events or 20 MB, whichever is smaller; rebase and upgrade records as their rows state), so undo survives a reload; a client with no record for a target (joined later, or the record left the cap) rebuilds it by replaying from its nearest checkpoint before the target or from the base and says it is slow; the replay keeps full records only for the events later undo events target (found by a pre-scan of the log) and touched sets for all; the replay is deterministic, so every client reaches the same state. | §2.9 undo section | undo; §11.3 clients |  |
| DETERMINISM-0471 | number | §7.4 | determinism | Saved undo section cap: last 64 events or 20 MB, whichever smaller (fallback 16 events). |  | DETERMINISM-0470; DETERMINISM-0474 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0472 | rule | §7.4 Truncation (unshared documents only) | determinism | When an unshared document's undo targets the event immediately before it, the engine may drop both from the log and hold the target on the session's redo stack; a redo re-appends the target with its original seq and advances nextSeq by one (the seq the kept log's redo would have spent), so every later event and every address a later user-created entity mints from its event's seq is the same in both logs; the canonical nextSeq (§2.9) never moves back; an undo removes its target's lifecycle entries and tombstones and a redo re-inserts them, both citing the target's seq (§2.8), so lifecycle block, archive and model digest of the truncated log equal those with the pair kept; the truncated target is held on the redo stack in the saved undo section (UndoRecord.redo, §2.9) so a redo after a reload re-appends it, and the packs it names stay in packs while held; a B-EDIT property test over random sequences folds each both ways (§6.5 (9)'s truncation cases among them) requiring equal model and records digests and equal addresses; a document attached to a host bridge never truncates. | §2.9 nextSeq, UndoRecord.redo | §6.5 (9) truncation identity (DETERMINISM-0233) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0473 | mechanism | §7.4 Checkpoints | determinism | Copy-on-write checkpoint (arenas chunked per district, cost only the chunks changed since the previous one) whenever replay cost accumulated since the last passes 80 ms at the session's rung, and before every epoch, rebase and upgrade, so any replay from a checkpoint stays ≤ 100 ms; a load taking §6.2 (iii)'s off-edit-path recomputation also takes one at h₀. | §3.3 arenas chunked | undo; §6.2 (iii); §7.5 upgrade undo |  |
| DETERMINISM-0474 | bar | §7.4 Checkpoints measurement | determinism | Measurement (M2a): undo of a CITY district regeneration ≤ 30 ms; undo memory ≤ 25 % of the model's working set after 100 edits; (M3a) the host-and-three-clients convergence test of §11.3 with interleaved undo and redo from every client; pre-decided fallback: the saved undo section shrinks to 16 events and checkpoints are taken every 40 ms of replay cost. | DETERMINISM-0473 | §16 M2a, M3a; §11.3 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0475 | test | §7.4 enumeration test | determinism | The enumeration test checks that every EditEvent kind — undo and redo included — has an exact inverse or an undo record (O9 d); save → load → save is byte-identical over the canonical form (O9 a). | EditEvent union | §13 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0476 | mechanism | §7.4 Warp and what stands on the ground | determinism | Seven kinds of record hold a world point and move with the ground they stand on: Pin.point (host: Pin.host's faces); Decoration.point (the L3 face containing it); the current position of a positional instance override (a move's to, an add's point, for a line layout the recorded generated point) held in the entity's override table (§8.10); a pinned label's Placement anchor and spine (its target's faces, or its target's corridor for a way, §9.1); LightSource.point (host: the face its host stands on on its host's arena — building footprint face; half-corridor strip of a way or bridge; square's face; gate's footprint; pin's host face; gallery's band; chamber's floor; a junction light's tunnel band faces (junction wedge and half-corridor strips); the faces of the underground element an access point's access_point vertex lies on for the light at its foot; a castle's inner ward, bailey and gatehouse footprint; a decoration's face; any other hand-placed light's host face; of several faces the one pointLocate returns); AccessPoint.ground.at (II.7.4; host: the ground face its ground.host names or stands on); the rings and lines of Works.before (§2.7, item 67; host: its target's plot face, or for a join or split the plot whose face holds the point). | §3.3 moved list; §2.7 records | warp; moveVertex; §5.3 light row |  |
| DETERMINISM-0477 | mechanism | §7.4 Warp re-point | determinism | After the kernel's commit the edit layer takes every such record whose host face has a moved vertex (the commit record's moved list, §3.3) and computes its new point from the move: the point's mean-value coordinates in its host face's outer ring before the move applied to the ring after it (+ − × ÷ and sqrt only, §6.5), rounded to the lattice; a record whose new point lies outside its host face, or nearer its boundary than its clearance (a pin's footprint radius, Decoration.keepClearCm, half an instance spacing, a label's box, a light's sizeCm; none for an access point's opening), is re-seated at the nearest lattice point of the host face inset by that clearance, ties by (y, x) (never a door light); if the inset is empty a generated pin, decoration, light or access point is re-placed by its placement rule in its host (§4.7 S11, §4.7 Light sources, §4.9, II.7.3) and a user's record (an override, pinned label, user-placed or edited pin, hand-placed light, an edit-placed access point) is orphaned and reported; a Works' kept rings and lines are drawn history: each point takes its mean-value image wherever it falls, never re-seated, re-placed or orphaned. | §3.3 moved; §6.5 arithmetic | warp lifecycle row (DETERMINISM-0425) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0478 | rule | §7.4 door light | determinism | A door light (standing beside its door on the footprint's ring, on the side §4.7's Light sources (4) draws) keeps its fraction of the ring edge nearest its point — the foot of its point on that edge clamped to [0, 1]; the nearer of two edges by squared distance, a tie to the edge first in the ring's order — its new point that edge's two end vertices where the move leaves them blended at that fraction (the mean-value image of a boundary point; + − × ÷ only), rounded to the lattice, so it stays within half a lattice diagonal of the moved facade whether the warp slides or turns it, the rounding never accumulating; never re-seated; goes with its ring edge even for a locked or pinned entity. | §4.7 Light sources (4); §2.2 rule 3 | §5.3 light row (DETERMINISM-0089) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0479 | rule | §7.4 Warp held records | determinism | A record of a locked or pinned entity (a door light excepted) is held like a held vertex: the warp's whole-step validation requires it to stay inside its host face at its clearance and halves the step otherwise (§3.6); the dry run lists it. A building's stored wing rings — with a stored stage-3 composition's rings, its plan's points (rooms, wall ends, bays, columns) and tower centres, and the building's basement ring — are carried with its footprint face in the same pass by §2.6's wing-carrying rule, and the whole-step validation includes §2.6's check through warp's recordCheck, halving the step when it fails: the basement's ring within its ground storey's wings (an unedited basement failing it re-derived by §2.6's basement decision, an edited one failing the step); for a stored composition its composed wings uniting to the moved face, its rooms inside the moved face united with the span rings of passages its wings span (J-A30.23), tower centres inside or on it. Every entity whose footprint, trace, deck or pier the move carried is re-based by §2.6's datum step (§7.3 (a) 4), a user-edited base kept; the undo record keeps every former point, wing ring, basement ring, stored composition and base; a replay recomputes the same by replaying the same operation; stamp and grid instances are world-anchored patterns, not displaced (§8.10); B-EDIT warp fixtures are §13.2's (J-DET-31; J-A68.73). | §3.6 recordCheck; §2.6 wing-carrying rule, datum step | warp; V02; V03 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0480 | table | §7.4 MFCG's recorded edit asks (IMPROVEMENTS O9 (f); B6; twelve users) | determinism | Each ask is a scripted B-EDIT scenario reporting its locality share and invariant results (§13.2); "expressible" counts working, measured edits: add a district → drawBoundary (S6 regroups covered cells; S8-S11 where the class band changed); delete a district → merge into its neighbour (S6 re-types; S8-S11 where the band changed); add roads → drawWay (S4 or S5 local operator; S8-S11 in split cells); remove roads → delete way (S5 regrows the merged cell or S4 re-routes; S8-S11); move / remove labels → moveLabel, hideLabel (LabelEntity.pinned/hidden only; 0 other model changes; S13b re-decides the labels whose boxes meet it); set district colours → setStyle (a thematic row keyed by the district's address; style only, 0 model changes); add / delete vertices → split (a way at a point), merge (edges), moveVertex (L1 edit; S8-S11 in touched cells); place the castle → setAttr(castle, site) (S4 re-routes the castle street; S7 re-traces when the castle is on the enceinte; cells under old and new reservations re-derive); size the castle grounds → setAttr(castle, baileyArea) (reservation resizes; cells re-derive; S7's run when on the enceinte); farms in chosen areas → setAttrMany (land-use faces under a drawn region → arable; S12 on those faces); draw walls → drawWall (S7 re-traces the run or adds an enceinte and cuts its strips; F-REDERIVE on plots in the new strips); draw bridges → drawWay class bridge across a river face (Bridge by §4.2 S1 (5)'s drawn-bridge rule; S4's operator lays the ends through its deck, pinned; deck row holds); drag points in the sea → moveVertex on the coast L0 (S1's water operator: moved coast is the new water, contours clipped, decks re-sited, footprint cleared, kept ways re-laid, others cut; S5 and S6; F-REDERIVE). | edit kinds; operators | §13.2 B-EDIT scenarios; §15 parity (B6) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0481 | rule | §7.5 (1) schema chain | determinism | migrate(doc, to) has two parts; (1) the schema chain — a sequence of pure, versioned transforms over the document format, forward only; a document whose schema is newer than the running library opens read-only with a message (view and export). | §2.9 schema version | §2.9 load; DETERMINISM-0171 records digest re-root |  |
| DETERMINISM-0482 | rule | §7.5 (2) upgrade — rebased base | determinism | The upgrade (a new output version) is an explicit, undoable upgrade event: the base and the whole ordered log (epochs included) replay under the new output version into a new model; a rebased base is checked then re-rooted — before the replay base.state is decoded under from and its model root compared with its root of record for from (§2.9 step 3; a mismatch refuses the upgrade with a message, the document staying at from), and the replay decodes it under to; the upgrade records the model root of that decoded state as baseDigest, which every later load under to checks. | §2.9 step 3 | DETERMINISM-0306; DETERMINISM-0457 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0483 | rule | §7.5 (2) mapping | determinism | The mapping: entities of the old model are matched to the new by kind, anchor within the kind's hysteresis radius, class family and, for faces and chains, overlap (IoU ≥ 0.5), through §6.3's matching routine and budget; a matched entity hands its address to its successor, and edits follow it only through §7.3 item 4's class-family check; nothing is matched by string equality. | DETERMINISM-0197 | upgrade |  |
| DETERMINISM-0484 | rule | §7.5 (2) confirmations | determinism | The fates and renames of the events before the upgrade are recomputed under to; the events whose recomputed values differ from the confirmed ones are named in changedConfirmations and their differences shown in the diff, once (§7.3 item 4). | DETERMINISM-0400 | MigrationMap.changedConfirmations |  |
| DETERMINISM-0485 | rule | §7.5 (2) packs saved | determinism (pack set §2.9) | Packs the new version stops shipping: before the upgrade commits, every pack of the pack set (§2.9) that to does not ship and packs does not hold is copied into packs from from's shipped copy (in the browser where from is the document's own version inside the window and its retained chunk carries its shipped packs, §6.4, and in the CLI), and the mapping names it in packsSaved; a copy that would pass §2.9's 2 MB cap is an F-EDIT conflict naming the pack and its size, nothing committed, the one resolution offered is keeping the old model (removing the pack cannot help, the pack set keeping every pack the log ever named, §2.9); such a town stays at its output version; once that version leaves the browser's window it opens as a frozen view whose frozenImport the same cap refuses (§2.9 step 2), viewable and exportable, and the CLI still replays it exactly (§6.4). | §2.9 pack set, 2 MB cap | F-EDIT; MigrationMap.packsSaved |  |
| DETERMINISM-0486 | number | §7.5 | model §2.9 | Pack/host-content cap: 2 MB (§2.9's cap). | §2.9 | DETERMINISM-0485; F-EDIT | restatement of §2.9 value |
| DETERMINISM-0487 | rule | §7.5 (2) orphans, orphaned locks, diff | determinism | An unmatched edit is a reported orphan, kept in the log and listed in the orphan table, never silently bound to another entity; an orphaned lock re-imposes its last geometry as a drawn constraint (a drawFootprint, drawWay, drawBoundary or drawWater event appended at the upgrade's position) unless the user drops it, so a locked building stays where it was; a diff (entities changed, orphans, changed confirmations, statistics deltas, and a per-epoch diff for a multi-epoch document) is shown before commit. | DETERMINISM-0483 | orphan table; V09 diff UI |  |
| DETERMINISM-0488 | rule | §7.5 (2) undo | determinism | The upgrade's undo record is its from output version, not a stored copy of the old model: undoing it replays the base and the log before the upgrade under from (the retained N−1 or N−2 chunk in the browser, any published version in the CLI, §6.4), reproducing the pre-upgrade model exactly, digest included (a rebased base checked against its root of record for from, §2.9 step 3); within the session the checkpoint taken before the upgrade makes it instant; when from has left the browser's window (library updated twice since) the undo is withdrawn there with a notice and the CLI command; the upgrade's diff stays viewable (J-DET-18). | DETERMINISM-0217; DETERMINISM-0473 | DETERMINISM-0457 | restated in §7.4 upgrade row (0457) |
| DETERMINISM-0489 | rule | §7.5 keep the old model | determinism | The user may instead keep the old model: the document stays at its output version while inside the retention window (§6.4); outside it the document opens as a frozen view whose one edit is a rebase (§2.9); FTG's "no way to regenerate old towns with new features" and "cannot be undone" are the weaknesses answered (J-DET-44, J-DET-54). | DETERMINISM-0217 | V09 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0490 | interface | §7.5 MigrationMap | determinism | MigrationMap { orphans: Id[] (targets of logged edits left without a successor, kept in the log, listed in the orphan table); packsSaved: { id; version; hash: Hash128 }[] (pack-set packs to does not ship whose content this upgrade copied into packs); changedConfirmations: number[] (seqs of events before the upgrade whose fates or renames recomputed under to differ from the confirmed ones) } — what the upgrade computed and showed; a replay recomputes it and reports a difference as for fates; address hand-offs are recomputed by §6.3's matching on every replay, never stored (the upgrade event stays small); its renames are EditBase.renames. | DETERMINISM-0483-DETERMINISM-0487 | upgrade event; §7.3 item 4 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0491 | test | §7.5 Test (M3a) | determinism | Upgrade tests (M3a): golden suite replays under N−1 and N−2; a corpus of edited documents upgraded across one output version keeps 100 % of edited entities or reports each orphan (O9 e); an upgrade undone (in session and after a reload) restores the pre-upgrade digest exactly; a METROPOLIS document's saved size does not grow with its upgrades beyond the upgrade events; a keptSnapshot-rebased CITY document upgraded across a test output version whose field driver and digest leaf encoding differ from from's opens editable after a reload and replays to the session's digest, and the upgrade undone restores the pre-upgrade digest; a document using a pack to stops shipping holds it in packs after the upgrade and reopens with it once from has left the window; a document whose such pack copy would pass 2 MB gets the F-EDIT conflict with only "keep the old model" offered and commits nothing; an upgraded document whose pre-upgrade corpus selection renames differently under to lists that change once in the diff and reloads with no mismatch report, while a divergence forced under the confirming version is still reported. | DETERMINISM-0481-DETERMINISM-0490 | §16 M3a; §13 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0492 | rule | §7.5 schema transforms 2026-09-25 | determinism (records §2.5-§2.7) | Schema transforms of items 11, 12, 17, 20, 28, 29, 30, 31 (pure, forward-only, checking the old records root and writing the new, §6.2): a plot without builtBand gets it from its DerivationRecord.band and its value companion from the record's field sample (exact for every document without an epoch; for one with epochs the band its last derivation read); fieldBand is that same band; a building's single entrance becomes entrances with one main entrance at that vertex (ordinal 0, nextEntrance 1), its basement derived at first load by §2.6 (an absent decision, never random); every building's use becomes a Use record (§2.7) with the building as only member and no formerUses; a building's households filled from its households' Household.building in address order, the retired occupants list dropped (J-A28.29); condition kept and reusedAs absent; a block's reach open (the parity presets' value); each plot's exposure derived at first load by §4.6's rule from its access rows (no older document has a passage, so no base wing carries spans), its knownTo and its buildings' defaulting from that exposure — an institution's optional knownTo stays absent and reads its plots'; a stored tunnel without headroomCm takes II.7.2's profile row for its kind and the network's preset, navigable derived; an older assignment gets existence 'map' and pinned false, its use linked at first load to the Use its member's building received; Composition.lod admits plan with no data change; a stored plan written before Room.scope and Room.unit gets each room's scope from its role's programme row (circulation for corridors and stair halls) and each room's unit at first load by §2.6's rule (one household: the whole shell is its unit); an elaboration cache is non-canonical and dropped at upgrade; no rejection, positive placement, DossierRef or Passage exists in an older document, so none is invented. | §2.5-§2.7 records; §2.8 DerivationRecord | schema chain (DETERMINISM-0481) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0493 | rule | §7.5 schema transforms 2026-09-27/28 | determinism (records §2-§4, §7.7) | Schema transforms of items 20 (h), 63-72, 74-82 and 101: a use, institution or assignment without concealment reads none, no front; an assignment without state reads active; an AccessPoint without state reads open, its knownTo derived at first load by II.7.3's rule, its source neighbourhood, and its under built from the older tunnel reference as part 0 and that tunnel; a placeAccess event's tunnel reads as its element, and so does the tunnel of an older convert event's underground decline op (silt, caveIn, seal, reopen; DeclineOp, §4.11 — flood is new and has no older form); a plot without tenure derives it at first load (item 70); a district without identity takes its character step's derived one at first load (§4.5), pressure 0, no nameHistory; an older recipe's character entries become its districtIntent entries field for field (wealth, density, age, function mix, planned / organic, greenery, fortification, building scale; the entry without a district the town-wide default); an older district record's stored character is dropped, the derived CharacterVector recomputed at first load (J-A72.21); a document without nextUndergroundPart takes 1 (part 0 the sewer's, §2.9); a Terrain without geology reads ordinary (§2.3); Document.dossier rebuilt from the log's dossier events at first load (item 74); an older event has no dossierChange, proposedBy, closure or points form, none invented; an older epoch event without a week takes its predecessor's week + round(52 × years) (§4.11's EpochInputs.week rule; the first epoch's predecessor week 0; item 76; J-A76.72); model data a later output version derives that an older document never held (Works records, rule-placed lights, the underground layer's other systems, galleries, chambers, links and undercity, clusters) appears only by an upgrade to that version, whose diff lists it, never by the schema chain. | §2-§4 records; §4.11 EpochInputs.week | schema chain; V04; V06; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0494 | rule | §7.5 Sugar events migrate by their expansion (J-A101.72) | determinism | A placeGate, quarter, roster or group event records the sugar.json version it was expanded with (expansion); every replay under the document's own output version expands it at that version (session and replay make the same events); an upgrade whose output version changed an expansion re-expands the event under to and hands its entities on by §6.3's matching like any re-derived scope; events whose expansion changed are named in the diff with the other changed confirmations (MigrationMap.changedConfirmations), once. | sugar.json; DETERMINISM-0351 | upgrade; DETERMINISM-0431 lifecycle row | restates 0431's migrate column |
| DETERMINISM-0495 | mechanism | §7.6 effectiveParams | determinism | effectiveParams = defaults(pinned table versions) ⊕ packs ⊕ preset ⊕ userGlobal ⊕ documentOverrides ⊕ log events, each layer a sparse delta with provenance; setting packs recorded in the recipe, or in the adjust that adds them, by id, version and content hash (§2.9); the UI shows which layer set a value and the report lists auto-resolved values with reasons (O16 a-b). | table versions; Recipe; log | every stage's parameter reads; GenerationReport.autoResolved (DETERMINISM-0111); V09 panel |  |
| DETERMINISM-0497 | rule | §7.6 Preferences | determinism | Preferences are folded in at creation: packs, preset, the user's global preferences and document overrides are copied into the recipe's params delta (shown as "from your preferences"); a later change to the machine's preferences never changes an existing document; applying them is the explicit action "apply my current preferences", logged as one adjust per changed parameter with its locality class. | Recipe params | DETERMINISM-0161 no machine preference; V09 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0498 | rule | §7.6 Packs | determinism (pack content §10.4, pack set §2.9) | A pack's content is §10.4's; its place in the pack set, retention in packs and a user-uploaded corpus as a pack are §2.9's; adding or removing a pack (selecting or deselecting a corpus included, §10.4) is an adjust of the recipe's pack list (param 'packs'): a Re-derive from the pack's first reader stage with the fate report (§7.1); when the pack changes a naming slice (a grammar row, or the corpus a culture or ancestry row field names) S13a re-draws exactly the unedited names citing a changed slice, listed for confirmation (§6.3 case (4)); a replay resolves every pack by hash from the document first (§2.9); a link is emitted only when every pack of the pack set is shipped, so never for a town whose history selects a user corpus (§6.4). | §10.4; §2.9 | adjust; §6.3; V11 content |  |
| DETERMINISM-0499 | rule | §7.6 Tables have keyed rows | determinism | Every rule table is versioned JSON: a map from a stable row key to a row whose cells carry { value, unit, provenance: { source, date, note }, calibratable, extension?: { over, mode } }; each table's schema declares its readers (the stages consulting it) and its slice kinds (row, weights, bracket, thresholds, match — §2.8). | §2.8 slice kinds | every stage; §13.9 calibration; V11, V12 |  |

relation templates, supply edges and designation templates (§10.2, §4.7); room-programme rows and layout patterns (§2.6, §10.2); the interior ladder's rows — households per shell, unit forms, shared and staff rooms, circulation and room-size bands per band (§2.6; item 28 (f)); the decline table and the survivors' ladder with durations per rung by direction and stock replaceability, churn caps, orderings, the middle-band designation and each rung's manifestation column (§4.11, §4.5; item 31); the class table's replaceability column (§10.2); hidden-share rows (§4.6); the island rule and sewer existence law (II.6, II.7); UndergroundRule and TunnelProfileRow (II.7.2, II.7.4); the disruption budget rows (§7.7); the entrance and cellar columns of the designation templates (§10.2, read by §2.6); RosterTables.basementGate (§10.2); prosperity.bands (§4.5); market.siting (§4.2 S3 (12)); FarmModeRow (§4.9); reach weights (§4.6); knowledge.admit (§9.2); cut.bendMode and cut.flattenM (§4.6); castle.minInnerM, wall.smoothPasses, the gates' waterfront share, district.growthRate (§4.2, §4.4, §4.5, §4.8); jitter.maxM and jitter.minM (§4.7 S11; jitter.minM's declared range starting above attractCm); the class-mix table and prestige rows (§4.7 S10b, §10.2); cluster rows and tenure defaults (§4.7 S11, §10.2); quarter identities, significance, power and sector links and band ranges with the dials' weights (§4.5, §10.2); the concealment column and cover table (§10.2, read by §4.7); underground system rows, UndercityRule, LinkRule, basement-access rows (II.7.3-II.7.6); WorksTable with its calendar row (§4.11); LightRule (lights.json; §4.7, §8.4); the palette's role values on the owner-opened targets (§8.2); sugar.json (§7.1); the dossier row sets tables/dossier/* (§14). | the named sections | §7.6 invalidation by slice; V11 content; V12 calibration | OMISSION-CANDIDATE: reroll.maxAdvance (0019) and crossing.searchBandM (§6.3) are called table values but are not in this list |
| DETERMINISM-0501 | rule | §7.6 naming slice | determinism | A naming slice of a culture or ancestry row is one field of it: a SliceRef of kind row with key <rowKey>/<field>, hashed over that field's value and, for a corpus field, the named corpus's content hash (a slice's hash changes when the corpus behind it changes); a naming draw cites only the fields it read, so a set on one corpus field (a user corpus pack's binding among them) changes exactly the names drawn from that field; every other slice stays a whole row, weight vector, bracket, threshold set or match set (§2.8) (J-DET-48). | §2.8 SliceRef | §6.3 case (4); naming records |  |
| DETERMINISM-0502 | rule | §7.6 Emulation first (item 23; J-A23.71) | determinism (owner rule) | Emulation first (owner: "based in our base of MFCG and FTG emulation first"): every rule's DEFAULT reproduces what MFCG or FTG does where they have the behaviour (§1.4's tiebreaker chooses where both do); extensions are layered above as data-driven tendencies with a chance or a weight, or options, never fixed laws or orders; a cell's provenance.source names where its value comes from in §17.1 (6)'s grammar — ref:⟨reference⟩ ⟨behaviour⟩ for an emulated default (or its (code) form with its P-id, item 62), otherwise own, architecture ⟨date⟩ or another of its values; an extending cell carries extension { over 'MFCG'\|'FTG'\|'both'\|'none'; mode 'chance'\|'weight'\|'option' }; the parity presets MFCG and FTG (the presets §13.6's side-by-sides run) set every extension cell's chance or weight to 0 and its option off (hidden-plot share, supply edges, upwind rows, island and sewer rules, the ladder's tendencies among them); with a parity preset on, B-REF's parity rows (§13.5) hold on the references' own behaviour; with the default preset the extensions run at their table values (§13.5's test). | §1.4 tiebreaker; §17.1 (6) grammar | §13.5, §13.6; V11; V12 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0503 | rule | §7.6 reading rule for absolutes | determinism | Reading rule for absolutes: where the document says a settlement "always", "never" or "first … then" does something, it means "by default, as a table value that can be changed per region, era and setting"; an engineering invariant (planarity, "never re-rolled", "never stored", a work limit) is not a settlement rule and stays absolute. |  | whole set (reading rule) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0504 | rule | §7.6 interior ladder defaults | determinism / V03 interiors | The interior ladder's rows (§2.6, item 28 (f)) are defaults in the same sense (a region, era or class may change what a band's households share and how small a poor unit gets); neither reference draws an interior, so the parity presets leave the plan rung alone: with no epoch and no relet every residential building houses what §4.7's generation fill gives it (at least one household, never above capacity), so the static baseline is unchanged. | §2.6; §4.7 | V03 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0505 | rule | §7.6 corpus content | determinism (owner release item 69) | No corpus content enters the design by the emulation rule except the palette bands the owner released on 2026-09-27 (item 69; §8.2's palette targets), whose role values carry provenance corpus with that release as evidence; the corpus phase re-signs every other table value through §13.9. | item 69; §8.2 | §13.9 calibration; V12 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0506 | rule | §7.6 Per-preset values (items 37, 43, 44; J-A37.71) | determinism | Where the reference's behaviour is a VALUE that differs from our default the cell carries presets?: { MFCG?: CellValue; FTG?: CellValue }, each with its own provenance (ref:⟨build⟩ ⟨behaviour⟩ (code) and its P-id where read in code); a parity preset resolves every cell in one order: its own presets entry; else for an extension cell 0 for a chance, weight or number and off for an option; else the cell's default; a categorical option cell has no neutral value, so the table schema refuses one without an entry for each parity preset; rows carried this way: cut.bendMode and cut.flattenM (§4.6 step 5 — MFCG preset straightFirst and 8 m; FTG reads the default); jitter.maxM and jitter.minM (§4.7 S11 — MFCG 4.8 m and 2 m; FTG reads 0, no recession, since no front-recession fact of the FTG client has been read, J-A43.71); the compound enclosure (§4.7 S11 — extension over 'both', its parity value stated there and read by both presets; no third preset for NG 1.2.2, whose §13.6 comparison compares fabric, J-A44.71); the light rows (§4.7, §8.4); §13.5's parity rows run under this resolution (T23-1). | §4.6 step 5; §4.7 S11 | §13.5 T23-1; V12 | restates §4.6/§4.7 preset values (8 m, 4.8 m, 2 m) |
| DETERMINISM-0507 | rule | §7.6 Overrides are row operations | determinism | An override records { op, rowKey, field, value, baseRowHash }; when a newer table version changes or removes that row the override is reported as an orphan — kept, not applied — with the old base row, the new row and the user's value side by side; an insert whose key already exists is an orphan too. | RowOp | editTable migrate (DETERMINISM-0446) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0508 | rule | §7.6 Invalidation by slice | determinism | A table edit changes the hashes of the slices it touches and exactly the entities whose recorded slices cite a changed slice re-derive (audited by the oracle, §7.3 item 7); recorded slices read from persisted DerivationRecord.slices for plots, blocks, buildings (§2.8), from persisted NamingRecord.slices for every generated name (a naming-table edit re-draws exactly the unedited names citing a changed slice, in a rebased document too), and from the provenance rebuilt by the last derivation or verification replay for every other kind; fallback — where neither exists (a kind without a derivation record in a document rebased from a kept snapshot or a frozen import) and for a row inserted into a table read by predicate (changing its match slice) — Re-derive from the table's first reader stage with the fate report, its confirmed fates stored in the editTable or editPreset event. | §2.8 records | editTable; DETERMINISM-0468 independence |  |
| DETERMINISM-0509 | rule | §7.6 Host tables; users and us | determinism | Host tables arrive as editTable, editPreset or setStyle events whose content is copied into the log with provenance host (source, date, version), so a document replays without its host (§11.4); users edit through the tables editor and presets dialog (preference layer); we change the system through new table versions — a declared shift whenever output changes (§6.4); the corpus phase replaces values and updates provenance without code changes; the default path with no edits is byte-identical to the shipped defaults (O10 h). | §11.4 | DETERMINISM-0230 (restated); §6.4; §13.9 | restates 0160 and 0230 |
| DETERMINISM-0510 | rule | §7.7 preamble | determinism | Three input layers above the parameter vector, all persisted in the recipe; a pointer names one as "§7.7 layer n", while "§7.7 item n" names the dossier contract's item n. |  | reference convention |  |
| DETERMINISM-0512 | mechanism | §7.7 layer 1 Intent | determinism | Intent (outcome targets; Intent type): form archetype, size as population / area / buildings with a tolerance, water setting and coast side, gate count, wall rings, planned ↔ organic, wealth level and gradient direction, era and region preset, defence level, density; Expansion (intent → ≈ 200 internal parameters) is a pure function held in data: each intent field maps to internal parameters by { param, fn 'const'\|'linear'\|'smoothstep'\|'table', args } rows (tables interpolate linearly) evaluated in a fixed order. | Recipe intent | §4 stages' parameters; §7.8 controls; T1 intend |  |
| DETERMINISM-0513 | rule | §7.7 layer 1 | determinism | Rows are continuous: no rounding and no comparison inside a continuous control; a discrete outcome is a probability drawn per entity or a discrete control that declares itself (§7.8). | DETERMINISM-0512 | §7.8 controls |  |
| DETERMINISM-0514 | table | §7.7 layer 1 example | determinism | Expansion examples: wealthLevel ∈ [0, 1] → field.characterOffset = lerp(−0.3, +0.3), granularity.joinMaxBonus = 2 · wealthLevel (fractional part = probability a candidate plot takes one more join, drawn from plot.join), greenery.parkPer1000 = lerp(0.1, 0.8); item 71's class mix reads wealthLevel itself (no expansion row), moving every band row's weights toward larger classes in a rich town and smaller in a poor one, cuts staying the town's own (§4.7 S10b; J-A71.42); planned ∈ [0, 1] → growth.headingSigma = lerp(20, 5) degrees per 100 m of street, growth.crossroadProb = lerp(0.02, 0.15), avenues.probability = smoothstep(0.3, 0.7, planned) per eligible tier-1 way. | DETERMINISM-0512 | §4.5 field; §4.4 growth; §4.7 S10b |  |
| DETERMINISM-0515 | rule | §7.7 layer 1 | determinism | Every internal parameter is documented (unit, range, effect, provenance, consuming stage) and reachable in the advanced panel (O10 a); the registry test proves 100 %. | control registry (§7.8) | §13 registry test; V09 advanced panel |  |
| DETERMINISM-0516 | rule | §7.7 layer 1 Surprise me | determinism | Surprise me draws every intent field from its declared range and weights on the intent.surprise stream of a fresh seed and writes the drawn intent into the recipe, so the town's link reproduces it. | intent.surprise concern | DETERMINISM-0317 | restates 0317 |
| DETERMINISM-0517 | rule | §7.7 layer 1 dossier fill | determinism (port V08) | For a dossier-bound map the port's creation half fills the intent from the dossier by the derivation table (item 10; wealthLevel from the dossier's prosperity word); the filled values are map facts (item 9) and stay editable. | derivation table (item 10) | V08 port; Intent |  |
| DETERMINISM-0518 | mechanism | §7.7 layer 2 Character and circumstances (items 72, 76-79) | determinism | The per-district intent DistrictIntent (wealth, density, age, function mix, planned / organic, greenery, fortification, building scale), supplied automatically per district and overridable, is the input shaping a district's generation; a district's character is item 72's derived profile (CharacterVector, §2.5), read from what was built and naming the district (§4.5), never an input; the circumstances are one CircumstanceSlice per entry of a timeline dated in weeks (§4.11's week; for a dossier-bound map the host's campaign week, item 76) that schedules epoch events: an epoch happens only as an epoch event in the log (§7.1, hook 8), proposed by the app at a timeline entry or by the port at §4.11's cadence; the timeline's first entry is the creation slice S0 reads (§4.2); the season is read from the week through §4.11's calendar row, never stored; an epoch's population target and terrain events ride its EpochInputs; a government's kind names its lordship (§10.2); the slice carries activity scalars (market, harbour, main-street, default 1) the prosperity field's kernels read under decline (§4.11); the recipe's feature set holds layers.islands, II.6's model setting (§7.8). | §4.5; §4.11; §2.5 CharacterVector | V01; V06; V08; §4.2 S0 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0520 | rule | §7.7 typed slices (items 77-79; J-A77.71) | determinism | The typed slices are read by item 72's dials and terms (§4.5) and by S2 and S3 (§4.2); each is recipe data at creation and epoch data afterwards; the port fills them from a dossier, and standalone the blueprint and our own rows fill them by the same rules, so every reader sees the same fields with or without a dossier. | CircumstanceSlice | §4.5 dials; §4.2 S2, S3; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0521 | mechanism | §7.7 Trade (item 77) | determinism | Trade partners in order: the dossier's named neighbours and partners its trade links name, each keyed by binding key (item 12); with none, one derived partner per through route of the settlement's access row (source 'derived'); standalone, one per external bearing the blueprint requests, else per through route of its access intent. Weight: a partner's listed trade links (a named neighbour with none counts one), zero for a hostile relationship or a suppressed economy (a rival's or cold-war partner's listed links kept, as SettlementForge keeps them), normalised to Σ = 1, derived partners sharing equally. Mode: where the site has navigable water, partners in weight order take sea (harbour) or river (docks) until their weights cover the water fraction of all toll and duty income (income rows the port marks water — port duties, river tolls — over all it marks water or road — gate and road tolls), the rest road; with no such income the water fraction is the site's default (1/2 harbour town, 1/4 river town; our own values, calibratable); without navigable water every partner is road. Bearing: the realm block's true bearing when sent (source 'realm'); else a draw on gate.pick keyed by the partner's key (§6.1), uniform over the arc the site admits for its mode (open water for sea, upstream and downstream reaches for river, the land arc for road). Road partners' bearings are among S3 (4)'s requested external bearings; every partner's bearing and weight feed TradeFlow (per-gate and harbour weights, dominant bearing), derived from the slice, entrepot and road-and-gate geometry at §4.3. North first: when true bearings arrive for a map drawn on derived ones the port first turns the geo anchor's bearingDeg (§11.1) to fit the derived gates to them (no geometry moves) and moves gates or roads only for the residual, as a dossier event under item 5's least-disruption budget (J-A77.72). | dossier neighbours, trade links; port income rows; §4.2 S3 (4) | TradeFlow (§4.3); §11.1 geo anchor; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0522 | number | §7.7 Trade | determinism | Default water fraction of trade: 1/2 for a harbour town, 1/4 for a river town (own values, calibratable). |  | DETERMINISM-0521 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0523 | mechanism | §7.7 Economy (item 78) | determinism | Economy shares over §10.2's RosterTables.sectors, Σ = 1: with a dossier its income sources mapped to sectors by the port's revenue rows (a revenue with no sector — rents — drops out and the rest renormalise; J-A78.71); standalone the blueprint's economy setting, by default an even mix amplifying no class (J-A78.72); read by item 72's economy dial (§4.5) at creation and each epoch past its hysteresis (§4.11) and by S2's town-level character (§4.2 S2, §10.2); the shares are read, never modelled (D-10, D-32). | §10.2 sectors; port revenue rows | §4.5 dial; §4.2 S2; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0524 | mechanism | §7.7 Powers (item 79) | determinism | One standing per power: with a dossier standing = its power ÷ the settlement's total, its kind by the port's category-and-name rows (military and arcane powers included, §2.7), governing and secret from the dossier; standalone the governing power the government row names and the established religion (§10.2), 0.6 and 0.4 (own defaults, calibratable); appliedStanding starts at standing and moves only past the dials' hysteresis at an epoch (§4.11); the base reads standings, never materialised factions (Faction stays the module's, §10.1); an arcane standing is honoured whenever a dossier states it and, standalone, arises only under a magic circumstance above none (D-3). | port category rows; §10.2 | §4.5 dials; §4.11; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0525 | number | §7.7 Powers | determinism | Standalone power standings: governing 0.6, established religion 0.4 (own defaults, calibratable). |  | DETERMINISM-0524 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0526 | rule | §7.7 undercity setting (item 65) | determinism / V04 underground | The undercity setting (auto by default) is require when the dossier lists a subterranean district-scale record (placed as a neighbourhood assignment II.7.5's existence test must host) and forbid when the blueprint or the host's circumstances rule one out; disorder (0-1, default 0) is the lawlessness II.7.5's size law reads (the blueprint's setting, a timeline entry's over epochs); the dossier's crime scalar never fills it — it reaches the undercity through §4.6's staged crime term alone (item 88; J-A88.41, J-A88.73), so crime is counted once. | II.7.5; §4.6 crime term | V04; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0527 | rule | §7.7 layer 3 | determinism | Constraints: pins and assignments (layer 3). | DETERMINISM-0529 |  |  |

(the size: any of the three); tolerance (relative); water: WaterSetting (§13.3's one spelling); coastSideCdeg?; gates?; wallRings?; planned (0-1); wealthLevel (0-1); wealthGradientCdeg?; era (era and region preset row, §10.2); defence (0-1); density (0-1, the density control §7.8) }. | forms catalogue (§7.7) | expansion rows; §4.2 S0-S3; T1 intend |  |

(one building/plot, one district, or for feature the way, bridge, quay or pin a named-feature claim names, item 16); dispersed? { count; kind: BuildingClass \| string; criteria }; criteria? (a building or neighbourhood assignment's own soft rows: a naming claim's location phrase); claim? { kind 'name'; bias: { identity (a quarter identity row §10.2); weight }[] } (names and biases, never zones); name?; function?; shapeClass?; drift: DriftBounds (± area %, ± floors, allowed layouts and roof kinds, class family — read against §4.7 S11's designation template, item 19); precedence 'overClassRules'; placed? (solver's placements, persisted with the event); existence 'map'\|'dossier' (dossier: exists on every map the module returns, item 20); dossier?: DossierRef; state 'active'\|'ruined'\|'removed' (item 80: written only by dossier events through item 14's rows; a map assignment stays active); memberState?: Record<Id, 'ruined'\|'removed'>; use? 'public'\|'local'\|'secret' (item 30 (d); a dossier fact when set; §9.2 reads it); concealment? 'none'\|'front'\|'hidden'\|'underground' (item 63); front? (a front the dossier names; absent: §4.7's cover table picks) }. | §4.7 S10b solver; §7.1 DossierRef | assign event; §4.7; §9.2; V08 |  |

| DETERMINISM-0531 | interface | §7.7 Rejection | determinism | Rejection { element; site; seq (the rejectSite event); held { epoch; populationTarget (the target in force when made: the last folded epoch's EpochInputs.populationTarget, else the population the recipe resolves to at S2) }; lapsedAt? (seq of the epoch or dossier event that lapsed it) } — model data in the digest (§2.8 lists it); the solver reads it as PlacementRequest.rejected while lapsedAt is unset. | rejectSite event | §4.7 S10b; lapse rule (item 3); §6.2 content layer |  |

| DETERMINISM-0536 | interface | §7.7 TradePartner, Sector, EconomyShares, PowerStanding | determinism | TradePartner { key (binding key or standalone name); mode 'road'\|'river'\|'sea'; weight (Σ = 1; 0 for hostile); bearingCdeg; relationship? (the dossier's word); source 'dossier'\|'derived'\|'realm' }; Sector = a row key of §10.2's RosterTables.sectors (trade, taxes and tolls, crafts, agriculture, extraction, fishing, finance, religion, military, magic, crime); EconomyShares = Record<Sector, number> (Σ = 1); PowerStanding { key; kind: Faction['kind']; standing (Σ = 1); governing; secret; appliedStanding (moves only past hysteresis at an epoch) }. | DETERMINISM-0521-DETERMINISM-0524 | §4.3 TradeFlow; §4.5 dials |  |
| DETERMINISM-0537 | interface | §7.7 DerivationRow | determinism | DerivationRow { row (DR1 …); family; covers (the §2.8 registry kinds and attribute paths the row derives); inputs (digest fields in the order read); rule (the section whose rule derives it); chance (a §6.1 concern or 'none'); bar (the coherence bar it holds, item 11) } — tables/dossier/derivation, provenance own. | §2.8 registry; §6.1 concerns | item 10 derivation table; V08; coherence invariant (DETERMINISM-0062) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0538 | interface | §7.7 DossierBinding, CarriedFacts | determinism | DossierBinding { line (the SettlementForge line, item 73); rows[] { dossierKey (binding key); entity (the address carrying Identity.dossier, §2.8, §6.3); kind; since { seq }; rebound? { from; seq; receipt }[] }; factHash: Hash128 (of carried); absorbed[] { seq; week \| null; factHash; changeIds } (every absorbed state newest last, item 5's convergence report) }; CarriedFacts = Record<binding key, the dossier fact's fields as the port's digest holds them> — the "previous dossier" item 1's diff runs against. | port; dossier events | Document.dossier; §5.3 binding invariant (DETERMINISM-0063); V08 |  |
