(shown beside the map's rule in the disruption report); receipts (receipt fields found, item 13) } — EditBase.dossierChange. | port diff | EditBase.dossierChange; ReportEntry.change; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0540 | interface | §7.7 ReceiptRow, StatusRow | determinism | ReceiptRow { row (F1 … F22); element kind; op; receipts (in precedence order: the first present decides cause, fate and week); response 'assign'\|'unassign'\|'setAttr'\|'status'\|'rebind'\|'epoch'\|'converge'\|'none'; dating 'receipt'\|'arrival' } — tables/dossier/receipts, provenance own, the SettlementForge line in its header; StatusRow { status; fates? (keyed first, item 14); response 'open'\|'wear'\|'dial'\|'smaller'\|'removed'\|'ruined'\|'none' } — tables/dossier/status. | port tables | item 13, 14; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0541 | rule | §7.7 Precedence | determinism | Precedence: lock > dossier existence > assignment > user-drawn geometry > rule tables > defaults — the one order for every meeting of protections and inputs (§7.2, §5.2 F-EDIT); S10-S11 apply it to assignments; a targeted assignment on a locked entity (or locked attribute path) is an F-EDIT conflict returned as an action (unlock, choose another target, or keep the lock); the dispersed solver never places a member on a locked entity; a host that must override a lock sends an unlock first, and the log records it (J-DET-11). | §7.2 | F-EDIT; §4.7 S10-S11 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0542 | rule | §7.7 Dossier existence | determinism | Dossier existence (with every dossier-owned value, item 11) sits below the lock and above every assignment, drawn shape and rule: a dossier-backed element is never removed by anything on the map and never placed on a locked entity — it takes the best unlocked site the hard rows allow and, when no site passes, its first closest option (fewest hard rows failed, then best score, in §4.7 S10b's order) with the rows it fails reported (F-SITING), never omitted (J-A20.71). | DETERMINISM-0541 | F-SITING (DETERMINISM-0041) | restated in F-SITING (0041) |
| DETERMINISM-0543 | rule | §7.7 One path — placement, then mutation (items 16, 18) | determinism | A blueprint's assignments and edits, every element of a dossier a new document starts from (the port's creation half, §14, item 73, builds that blueprint and prefix through the derivation table, item 10, the recipe's seed derived one way from the dossier's, §14, item 85) and the declarations of a skeleton file imported as a new document (§11.1, item 101) become assign and edit events at log positions 0…k, the assign events flagged creation (S2 reads them ahead as fixed rows, J-A22.74: the dossier's elements counted in S2's demand, their land claimed eagerly at S7b and placed claims-first by S10 (3), J-A22.72; a skeleton's drawn declarations read ahead by the stages that make what they declare, §4.1); every assign event (blueprint, dialog, host bridge or host door §11.4) is placed by §4.7 S10b's one solver for its scope (claims-first at 0…k, least-disruption order after the city is built) and applied by §4.7 S11's designation mutator, so an assignment gives the same town whichever way it arrived and never re-rolls what stood there. | §4.7 S10b, S11; §14 port; §11.1 skeleton | creation prefix (DETERMINISM-0159); V08; V10 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0544 | rule | §7.7 Re-solving | determinism | When a member of a dispersed assignment is superseded without a successor, only that member is re-placed, with every mapped placement as a fixed point (minimum spacing measured from them); nothing still mapped moves. | §4.7 S10b | assign lifecycle migrate (DETERMINISM-0437) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0545 | rule | §7.7 Epochs (item 17 (d); J-A17.71) | determinism / V06 | An assignment is a stable identity that moves with its use under §4.11's use step unless pinned (then it stays and the town adapts); members are added only by an event changing the count or criteria, then placed with existing members fixed; every move, closing and reopening is reported (§5.4 changes, §11.3). | §4.11 use step | ReportEntry; V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0546 | rule | §7.7 dossier contract item 1 One way (items 20, 22) | determinism (port V08) | One way (owner: "the map does not edit the dossier … This module only takes input from SettlementForge and returns back the map"): SettlementForge → map module → map; the dossier enters through §11.4's host door as dossier events (assign, unassign, setAttr with author 'host', EditBase.dossier and EditBase.dossierChange set) in the host's order; the module never writes to, patches or proposes a change to the dossier and the bridge has no message that does (§11.3); map edits live in the map's own document (§2.9), which the host may store as an opaque artifact; nothing flows back. | §11.4 host door | V08; §11.3 bridge |  |
| DETERMINISM-0547 | mechanism | §7.7 item 1 port diff | determinism / V08 port | The port produces the events (§14, item 73): SettlementForge sends whole dossiers and no change list, so the port diffs the incoming dossier against the dossier facts the map carries by binding keys (Document.dossier.carried, item 12), writes one DossierChange per changed element (item 13), dates it by §4.11's dating rungs (item 76) and emits its events. | CarriedFacts; §4.11 dating rungs | dossier events; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0548 | rule | §7.7 item 1 The map never asks (item 20 (h) (iv); J-A20.79) | determinism (owner rule) | The map never asks (owner: "it is one directional"): a gap in the dossier is the map's to fill by the derivation table (item 10), never a request — no message, report or field asks the host for a fact; the doors (§11.4) and the port declare OPTIONAL inputs used when present (HostContext, §14), never a required one beyond what the dossier holds today. | derivation table | §11.4; §14 HostContext; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0549 | rule | §7.7 item 1 What the port never reads (item 86; J-A86.71) | determinism / V08 port | Ignore-list: the port never reads a host's own map-side layers (urbanFabric, mapEdits, fogSessions, every worldState.spatialLedgers.* sub-ledger, the spatial substrate included), its derived reads (district profile, map profile, causal state, system state, capacities, daily life, threat profiles, the spine), its generation log (simulationTrace), aiOverlays, generationCoherenceReceipt, its generation sliders (config.priority*), its NPC social groups, and prose beyond a description seed the clerk may read (item 101); the campaign's frozen spatialDigest is the one campaign fact the optional realm block may carry (§14); the adapter reports every key it dropped (ignored_key, §14). Reasons: a host's map memory would make its map program the base (against the owner's rule that what we build stays the base); the sliders' effect already sits in the institutions and income shares (reading them would count it twice); SettlementForge's own district names (in its news) are never matched to our districts (owner, J80 Q6; J-A86.72). | SettlementForge dossier keys; §14 adapter key list | V08; wiring dossier trace (ignore-list) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0550 | rule | §7.7 item 2 Existence | determinism | Everything the dossier specifies (buildings, institutions, assignments, named features) is a dossier-backed assignment existing on every map the module returns, live or (when the dossier lists it ruined) present as its ruin (item 14): F-SITING never drops it, delete never removes it, only a dossier event removes, adds or re-designates it; a removal (state removed) leaves the plot vacant through closeUse for item 17's rules to re-use; the dossier's population is a dossier fact — a count the households filling it honour (they are map facts, derived and housed through the roster's demand, §10.2). | Assignment.existence/state | §5.3 dossier row; F-SITING; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0551 | rule | §7.7 item 2 map-event refusal list | determinism | A map event (appended without EditBase.dossier by a user or a host's map edit) that would close, remove or re-designate a dossier-backed element is refused as F-EDIT with rejectSite (move it) and placeAt (designate the plot for something else, the element re-placed): an unassign; a setAttr of its use or class; a convert closeUse of its use or mutate of its plot to another designation; a conversion emptying or removing the building housing it (vacate, abandon, decay, ruin, robStone, clear, demolish); a plot join or split whose fate would demolish that building; a map delete is recorded as rejectSite (item 3); a relocateUse or swapUse moves it and is not refused (J-A20.75). | edit kinds | F-EDIT (DETERMINISM-0051); dry run (DETERMINISM-0347) |  |
| DETERMINISM-0552 | rule | §7.7 item 2 epochs | determinism / V06 | An epoch is bound by the same rule without being refused by it: no step of an epoch closes a dossier-backed use or leaves one without its building — §4.11's Dossier content across epochs relocates it first or keeps it in its building wherever a step would close it or empty or remove that building — so every dossier-backed element is live and placed (or present as its listed ruin) after every epoch (§5.3's row); elements the generator added on its own (filler houses, trees, lanes) may be deleted on the map with local repair, and whatever the dossier needs of them (housing for its population, a demanded class) is re-provided elsewhere by the same solver (J-A20.72). | §4.11 Dossier content across epochs | §5.3 dossier row (DETERMINISM-0061); V06 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0553 | rule | §7.7 item 3 Rejection, not deletion | determinism | A map "delete" of a dossier-backed element is the rejectSite event: the site excluded (Rejection; PlacementRequest.rejected), the solver re-places the element by the least-disruption order (§4.7 S10b), surroundings repair locally (§7.3), same seed; the rejection is not permanent (owner: "it is not permanent because if a settlement grows or declines that might be appropriate to put something there"): it holds while the settlement stays in its state and lapses (site eligible again, the element staying where it is) at the first epoch event folded after it whose populationTarget differs from held.populationTarget by more than the roster tolerance (§4.11; item 76; J-A76.43, J-A76.71), or at the first dossier event folded after it whose effective scope holds the site's block; the lapsing event writes lapsedAt inside its own touched set (no event logged for the lapse); an undo of a lapsing epoch that carried no dossier event restores the hold (J-A20.74, J-A20.41); a user lifts a rejection with rejectSite op 'lift'. | Rejection; §4.11 roster tolerance | rejectSite lifecycle (DETERMINISM-0438); V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0554 | rule | §7.7 item 4 Positive placement | determinism | Positive placement (owner: "maybe you want a specific place to be something"): placeAt pins a dossier-backed element, an assignment member or a designation the generator controls to a chosen plot; the solver honours it when the hard rows allow (joining plots and mutating buildings by §4.7 S10 (7) and S11, relocating whatever was there by the least-disruption order), else F-EDIT with the blocking rows and closest acceptable sites; it never creates or deletes dossier content, persists until the user lifts it or the dossier removes the element, and draws on siting keyed by the plot and the event (§6.1). | §4.7 S10b, S10 (7), S11 | placeAt; F-EDIT |  |
| DETERMINISM-0555 | rule | §7.7 item 5 Re-application | determinism | A dossier change after the first map mutates the CURRENT map, never reshuffles: the solver's least-disruption order (vacant or under-used plots first, then a use that can move within the same district, one displacement level, then a join only when the new use needs the land — §4.7 S10b) finds the site, S11's mutator adapts buildings, a removal vacates; households the dossier adds fill standing shells first by relet up to §2.6's capacity before any new plot (item 28 (f), §4.11), plans re-cut per §7.3; positive placements and the map's other edits are honoured first as pins and protected entities (§7.2) and the dossier event applied around them; an edit it can no longer honour is reported and orphaned, never dropped silently (J-A20.73); its disruption (every moved use, joined or vacated plot and mutated building with its causing rule) is computed in the dry run against the request kind's DisruptionBudgetRow, stored in EditBase.disruption and shown as its fate report; the budget bounds the search (the first rung of the order that fits is taken) and the effective scope, never the dossier's authority: an event no rung fits within budget still lands at the least-disruptive site, reported over budget (J-A22.73); everything outside the disruption set keeps its exact geometry and identity (oracle §7.3 item 7 (vii)). | §4.7 S10b order; DisruptionBudgetRow | §7.3 item 1; oracle (vii) (DETERMINISM-0408); V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0556 | rule | §7.7 item 5 A host's undo (item 87; J-A87.71) | determinism / V08 | A host restoring an earlier dossier state sends it as a dossier change like any other; the port diffs it, and because §4.7 S10b's least-disruption order takes vacant plots first and §4.11 prefers a building's formerUses, an element removed then restored returns to its own vacated building and an element added then withdrawn leaves the plot it took — the map converges to its earlier state wherever nothing else changed; the residue is reported and the report names the earlier absorbed state the dossier now equals (state_matches_absorbed, by the binding's absorbed hashes, item 12); no rewind of dossier events exists; a dossier event stays no undo target. | DossierBinding.absorbed | V08; §7.4 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0557 | rule | §7.7 item 6 Continuity | determinism | The map is a function of the seed, the ordered history of dossier events and the map's own edits (§6.2); the same history replays exactly; two histories reaching one dossier may give different valid maps (continuity over path-independence; owner: the seed should "respect the previous layout prior to the addition or subtraction", J-A22.71); a brand-new map from an existing dossier has every element present from the start (One path); inside the evolution stage the same rules apply; a dossier change the host sends with an epoch rides in EpochInputs.dossier and is applied at §4.11's step (e) after the use step and ladder and before siting (J-A22.42); one sent between epochs folds at its own log position and mutates the map the last epoch left (item 22 (e)); the events an epoch carries take their seq from nextSeq when the epoch event is appended (the epoch's own first, then the carried events' in the host's order), so their draws and minted addresses are keyed as a standalone event's (§6.1, §6.3); they fold only inside the epoch and are never undo targets, nor is the carrying epoch (§7.4) (J-A22.76); the port groups a host's changes by §4.11's cadence (item 76): an element change folds at its own week as a standalone dossier event in the host's order; a population change or typed-slice change rides the epoch the cadence schedules, in its EpochInputs. | §4.11 step (e), cadence | DETERMINISM-0177 (restated); EditBase.seq; V06; V08 | restates 0177 |
| DETERMINISM-0558 | rule | §7.7 item 7 Knowledge | determinism | A dossier may designate an institution hidden or secret and, for a clandestine one, its concealment and front (item 63): Assignment.knownTo, concealment and front are then dossier facts; the solver prefers hidden plots through the class's relation rows (§4.6, §4.7 S10b; item 30), places a front and the function it hides as one item by §4.7's concealment rule, and §9.2's label policy reads them; a raid or discovery is a dossier event applied as a least-disruption mutation (the front may close, the hidden function relocate, a passage be sealed; items 17, 22, 30); a map delete relocates by rejectSite; a map setAttr of a dossier-set knownTo, concealment or front is refused as dossier-owned (item 11; J-A20.77, superseding the override clauses of J-A30.71 and J-A30.72); the drawing's display stays the user's (a label's display text and visibility §9.1; each viewer's knowledge key §11.3); where the dossier sets no such value it is a map fact edited as §7.1 lists. | §4.7 concealment; §9.2 | F-EDIT; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0559 | rule | §7.7 item 8 What is staged | determinism / V08 SettlementForge | The port's creation half is the base's (M1, exercised by the frozen dossier fixtures of §13.2 — items 83, 84 — never by a live host); its absorb half lands with this contract at M3a; its wiring to a live host bridge is the owner's SettlementForge stage (§11.4, §14, Staged by the owner's roadmap; item 73); no door opens the other way (the host's simulator never reads the map's geometry, J86, §11.4); records and rows the port will fill when a host supplies them are declared schema-complete and empty in the base: ConditionOverlay, DepositSite, HistoryHint, PowerRelation and the dossier-backed Person (§2.7), and the impairment, hinterland, crime, custom-weight and legitimacy rows (§10.2) (item 88); everything else in this contract (events, records, precedence, rejection, positive placement, budget, continuity, items 9-16) is the base's, exercised today through the dialog, the API and the fixtures. | §16 M1, M3a; §13.2 fixtures | V08; §14; §16 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0560 | rule | §7.7 derivation rule preamble (item 20 (h); items 74-87) | determinism (owner rule) | Owner: "whatever the dossier doesn't provide, the rest of the map code must compensate. it is one directional. only the dossier is the source of immutable truth while the rest can be constructed at whim. better if the rest of the map code coherently derives things from the dossier and in accordance with our rules". | owner rule 20 (h) | items 9-16 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0561 | rule | §7.7 item 9 Two tiers of fact (item 20 (h) (i)) | determinism | Every fact is either a dossier fact (a record or value the dossier supplies, carried under its binding key, immutable on the map, changed only by a dossier event) or a map fact (everything else: positions and geometry, plots, buildings and their classes, the households filling the dossier's population, lot assignments, prosperity values, the settlement-level trade direction and partners' derived bearings, a collective record's count inside its range, names the dossier does not give, each district's character), generated, regenerated, edited and replaced freely subject only to items 10 and 11; dossier facts by kind: the settlement's name, tier word and population; each dossier-backed element's existence, state (item 14), name, kind and membership and, where given, knownTo, concealment and front; a collective record's count range; each quarter's name (item 16); the typed slices' dossier-sourced values (partners, relationships and listed links, income shares, power standings, the government); the conditions; for every field §2.8's factTier derives which tier holds it (never stored; J-A20.23), read by §7's edit rules (item 11), §9.2's reader, §11's exports and the API through which any clerk reads (§11.2); an author's declarations (item 101) are map facts factTier reads as declared, held until the author changes them and never above a dossier fact (a contradicting declaration refused as dossier-owned with the closest acceptable alternatives, item 20 (g)) (J-A20.76). | §2.8 factTier | §9.2 reader; §11 exports; §11.2 API; V08; V10 clerk |  |
| DETERMINISM-0563 | rule | §7.7 item 10 Derive before invent (item 20 (h) (ii); J-A20.78) | determinism | A map fact the dossier does not supply is derived from what it supplies by the design's rules before any recourse to chance; the derivation table is an INDEX: one row per map-fact family — the dossier inputs it reads (the port's DossierDigest fields, §14), the section whose rule derives it (stated there, never here), where seeded chance enters (a §6.1 concern or none) and the coherence bar it never contradicts (item 11); a row's inputs are read in the order written (a host's optional input when present (HostContext, §14), then the dossier's, then the blueprint's own), the rule's default when none is present; a missing input never becomes a request; standalone the blueprint and our own roster are the inputs of every row (the same rows: connecting a dossier changes inputs, not rules); tests run the rows from M1 on frozen dossier fixtures and blueprints alike (§13.2, items 83, 84); the rows are data (DerivationRow; tables/dossier/derivation, provenance own), each naming the §2.8 registry kinds and attribute paths it covers; §13.2's totality test proves every map-fact family of the registry is covered by a row and every row's chance names a registered concern or none (item 20 (h), item 83's vocabulary test). | §14 DossierDigest, HostContext | §13.2 totality test; V08 |  |

| DETERMINISM-0574 | rule | §7.7 item 11 Coherence (item 20 (h) (iii); J-A20.77) | determinism | The derivation obeys four rules, proved by §13.2's coherence bars on the design's own dossier events and by the port's bars on the frozen dossier fixtures (item 83), checked at run time by §5.3's coherence rows: (a) no derived fact contradicts a dossier fact (counts, kinds, existence and state, membership, realm-level trade facts — each table row's bar); (b) a dossier change re-derives exactly the dependent map facts (rows whose inputs it changed) inside its disruption set by item 5's least-disruption mutation, never a re-roll; (c) the derivation reads nothing beyond the seed, the ordered dossier history and the map's edits (item 6); (d) nothing derived flows back (item 1); the rules bind the DERIVATION (generation, re-derivation, every dossier event); a user's edit of a map fact (a prosperity stroke, a moved building, a renamed lane) stands; an edit that WOULD alter a dossier fact (a rename of a dossier-named element or quarter, a change of its kind or class, a setAttr of a dossier-set knownTo, concealment, front, state, a collective count outside its range, an adjust of a dossier-bound population or dossier-supplied slice value) is refused as F-EDIT dossier-owned, the map-side alternative offered where one exists (label display text §9.1; a count inside the range). | derivation table | §5.3 coherence row (DETERMINISM-0062); F-EDIT; §13.2 coherence bars |  |
| DETERMINISM-0575 | rule | §7.7 item 12 The binding (item 20 (h) (v), item 74; J-A74.72) | determinism / V08 adapter | SettlementForge keys most records by NAME and gives most institutions no id at birth, so the map binds its own stable addresses (§6.3) to the dossier's keys in the document's dossier block (§2.9: DossierBinding and CarriedFacts), written by the port at creation and every absorb; the KEY LAW is stated here and applied by the port's adapter (§14), the only code reading SettlementForge's fields. | §2.9 dossier block; §14 adapter | V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0576 | table | §7.7 item 12 key law | determinism | Binding keys: settlement → settlement:<id> (the dossier's own id, stable across reloads, same-seed regeneration and section rerolls; a new id is a new dossier and a new map; receipt rename-settlement renames the title label only); institution → inst:cat:<catalogId>, else inst:uid:<localUid>, else inst:name:<slug(name)> (SettlementForge's anchor precedence; #k appended for a collective record's k-th member; two records of one key told apart by order in the dossier, reported duplicate_key; receipts demotedFrom with fate demoted_by_disaster, promotedFrom with upgraded_by_reconstruction: row kept, record re-classed by its new name); power → power:id:<faction.id> when authored, else power:<slug(name)> (receipt rename-faction); notable → npc:<id> (a successor's replacedNpc links its row to the one replaced); condition cond:<id>; resource res:<key>; quarter quarter:<slug(name)>; government government (no receipts). | SettlementForge dossier fields | DossierBinding.rows; DossierRef.element; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0577 | rule | §7.7 item 12 | determinism / V08 | slug is the line's own name normalisation applied by the adapter (a name key agrees with the catalogue key the line writes at birth); class is read apart from identity (DR24): a record's class comes from its current NAME when a demotion or upgrade receipt is present (its kept catalogue key naming the old kind), else from its catalogue kind, else from its name, category and tags; a kind with no class row takes its category's generic class, reported unmapped_kind, never refused (at M1 the rows cover M1's classes); re-binding only on a receipt of the table: a name change without one is a removal plus an addition, reported rename_without_receipt (J-A74.73); a power's controlled-institution list resolved to binding keys through the stable part the line stores (slug of the institution's own id, else of its name) against the current roster, never through the catalogue key; DossierRef.element is the binding key and DossierRef.version the SettlementForge line; Identity.dossier carries the ref on the entity (§2.8; J-A20.22); a re-binding re-points a binding row and never re-keys a map address (§6.3); the model digest does not cover the block (the model carries each entity's current key in Identity.dossier, and carried holds dossier facts the map does not draw, e.g. a notable's role, whose change must not change the digest); binding rebuilt from the log's dossier events at load and must equal the stored rows (§5.3); factHash is the hash of carried (J-A74.71). | §2.8 Identity.dossier; §6.3 | §5.3 binding row (DETERMINISM-0063); §6.2 exclusion (DETERMINISM-0168); V08 | restates 0201 (§6.3) and 0168 |
| DETERMINISM-0578 | rule | §7.7 item 13 change envelope and receipt table (item 75; J-A75.71) | determinism / V08 | Every event a dossier change generates carries its DossierChange in EditBase.dossierChange so the disruption report, LifecycleEvent.cause and §5.4's changes say why (host's reason beside the map's rule, item 22 (b)); SettlementForge records a change only through scattered receipts (stamps, capped history lists, decrees, a news feed), so the port reads them through the RECEIPT TABLE (ReceiptRow; tables/dossier/receipts): per change kind the receipt fields in precedence order (the first present decides cause, fate and week by §4.11's dating rungs) and the response; the design states the ten rows the survey's verification corrected and F22 (item 87's undo row); the others — F6 reopened, F7 population, F9 prosperity, F10 power standings, F11 government, F12 conditions, F13 resources, F16 deity, F17 notables, F19 a confirmed table event, F21 a reroll — are data rows of the same type, their completeness §13.2's vocabulary totality test (item 83); a change no rung dates is applied finished without transition stages, reported unreceipted_change. | ReceiptRow; §4.11 dating rungs | LifecycleEvent.cause (§2.8); ReportEntry; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0579 | table | §7.7 item 13 receipt rows | determinism / V08 | F1 institution added (a new binding key; createdByEventId or an add-institution decree; _worldPulseTierAdded, _worldPulseEconomyBuilt or _worldPulseFounded with outcome ids and foundedAt; the fate founded_by_flourishing writes nothing else, dated by arrival alone) → an assign with existence dossier placed by S10b's dossier request; an addition under a key carried as removed or ruined is a REOPEN (F6, item 14); a missing foundedAt never reads as "stood since the founding". F2 removed (worldPulseFate and _worldPulseInactive FIRST — closure fates hollowed_out, abandoned, bankrupt, closed_for_want_of_custom, shuttered, abolished, disbanded, captured_by_local_powers, abandoned_with_the_settlement under status remnant — then status removed with removedByEventId, or a remove-institution decree) → item 14's removed: unassign, closeUse, plot vacant. F3 reduced or converted (fates privatized, downsized, reduced_to_watch_post, survives_as_remnant; demotedFrom or promotedFrom) → setAttr of the designation in place (item 18 mutation); demotion or upgrade is also F15's re-binding. F4 impaired or restored (visible impairments[], state impaired derived) → physical types (capacity, infrastructure, access, supply) one condition step per severity band; non-physical (legitimacy, influence, wealth, staffing, corruption) the institution's own dial term only (§4.5 (6); J-A88.42). F5 ruined (status ruined with destroyed_by_disaster, remnantReason and the calamity's targets; never destroyedByEventId, the settlement's) → item 14's ruined. F8 tier moved (a tierHistory[] entry, or a bare tier change with none) → rung re-read at the next epoch's target (§4.11); the tier word re-titles the map (item 15). F14 trade (_cutRoutes, _userRoutes, a route link, tradeLinks, a partner's relationship; rival's or cold-war partner's links kept, only hostile or suppressed economy carrying none) → the trade slice at the next epoch. F15 rename (rename-settlement, rename-faction, rename-npc; for an institution only demotedFrom or promotedFrom) → item 12's re-binding; without a receipt a removal plus an addition, reported. F18 decree (decrees[] applied with tickRef; the week's decreeCauses[]) → the decree's own change rows; a decree whose verb binds no world effect expects no change. F20 settlement ended or born (destroyedByEventId on the root; lifecyclePatch; memberBirths) → an END is an epoch ruining every building by the decline hooks (§4.11); a resettlement of the same site is the SAME map regrowing on its old plan by item 8's regrowth-first order (owner, J80 Q4; J-A75.72); a member birth is a new dossier and a new map. F22 host undid a change (incoming fact hash equals an earlier absorbed state's) → item 5's forward convergence, reported state_matches_absorbed. | SettlementForge receipt fields | tables/dossier/receipts; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0580 | table | §7.7 item 14 A listed element's state (item 80; J-A80.71, J-A80.72) | determinism / V08 | SettlementForge never deletes a record, so an assignment carries state written only by dossier events through StatusRow rows (tables/dossier/status), keyed on the fate word and inactive flag BEFORE the status (a bankrupt shop never kept open as a smaller designation): absent or active → open; impaired (derived from visible impairments) → open, physical types wear the building one condition step per severity band, non-physical reach the institution's own dial term only (§4.5 (6)); remnant with privatized, downsized, reduced_to_watch_post or survives_as_remnant → a smaller designation in place (item 18 mutation); remnant with a closure fate (F2's list) or removed → removed: unassign, closeUse, plot vacant (item 2); ruined, destroyed, destroyed_by_disaster, ruined_by_decree → ruined: PRESENT as its ruin, use closed, building ruined (§2.8 existence), counted present by §5.3's dossier row; vacant (an empty seat) → no physical change. A listed ruin stays on the map as its ruin (never contradicted by a map showing it open or gone); an epoch keeps it as it stands — the decline ladder never robs, clears or rebuilds its building (§4.11's exemptions) until a dossier event changes its state; a reopen (to active) re-opens its use in its own former building first (S10b's dossier request ranks it first, §4.7), repaired by a Works where item 67's clock runs (§4.11) and at once otherwise; a collective record's state is distributed over its placed members: the affected share = the severity band's share (all members when no severity), members chosen on siting keyed by the assignment with the dossier event's seq as creation index, recorded in memberState; the record's state stays active while any member is. | StatusRow; §2.8 existence | §5.3 dossier row; DR25 (DETERMINISM-0573); V06; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0581 | rule | §7.7 item 15 Population, rung and tier word (item 81; J80 Q3; J-A81.71, J-A81.01, J-A81.72) | determinism | A dossier whose population lies below 1 500 is refused by the port (E_RUNG_UNAVAILABLE, naming the village product, PART III) while no village product is registered: §1.1's clamp to a 1 500-person TOWN applies only to a blueprint request carrying no dossier; a dossier above the 120 000 cap is refused the same way (E_RUNG_UNAVAILABLE naming the cap, nothing absorbed); both refusals hold at creation and every absorb (§14's creation and absorb); §1.1's clamp to the cap serves only a request without a dossier; inside the ladder the population chooses the rung (§1.1) at creation and at each epoch's target (§4.11); the dossier's own tier WORD is a dossier fact and the map's title word (§9.5) — a "city" of 5 001 people is drawn on the TOWN rung and still titled a city; a bare tier change with no history entry fires row F8 like any tier receipt. | §1.1 size ladder; PART III village | §14 port refusal; §5.4 (DETERMINISM-0121); §9.5 title; V07 village; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0582 | number | §7.7 item 15; §5.4 | determinism | Dossier population floor 1 500 persons (TOWN floor) and cap 120 000 (metropolis cap); a dossier outside [1 500, 120 000] is refused with E_RUNG_UNAVAILABLE while no village product is registered (floor side). | §1.1 | DETERMINISM-0121; DETERMINISM-0581 | stated twice in unit (§5.4, §7.7 item 15) |
| DETERMINISM-0583 | rule | §7.7 item 16 Quarters and landmarks — naming claims (item 82; J80 Q5, Q6; J-A82.71-73) | determinism | SettlementForge picks its quarters by a name test at generation and never refreshes them, so a quarter enters the map as a NAMING CLAIM, never a zoning: a neighbourhood assignment with claim set, carrying the quarter's name, a kind bias from the quarter rows (tables/dossier/quarters: Market → market identity; Religious → temple; Noxious Trades → craft with noxious rows; Waterfront → docks or riverbank by its phrase; Government → the seat's district; Mages' → arcane identity; Artisan → craft; Shadows → a low band and larger hidden share; Wealthy and Common Residential → band biases; Roadside Shrine → a shrine claim with a soft edge row; the village quarters Alehouse & Common, Fishing Landing, Woodcutters' Ground one row apiece), and its location phrase as SOFT rows in criteria ("Downstream/downwind" → downstream, downwind; high-ground phrases → highGround; "Central" and "Village centre" → nearCentre; water phrases → nearWater; "Between market and residential" → nearMarket; "At the forest edge" → toward the wood land use; "Isolated area (safety concerns)" → far from the centre; "Old town or slums" → the oldest stage and a low band; "At the settlement edge" → the edge; "Spread throughout" → no district; "Eastern district (traditional)" → a soft east row at low weight, a placeholder dropped on the owner's word); at creation S6's typing reads the claim's bias and rows as soft terms (§4.5); the district whose emergent identity best matches the claim carries the name (protected as an assigned name, §10.4, kept past item 72's hysteresis); when no district matches above the claim floor the name waits, reported, re-matched at each epoch, never forced; an ABSENT quarter is never a fact; SettlementForge's own district names (its news) are never matched; a landmark string the roster already demands becomes a NAMED-FEATURE claim (granularity feature: the way, bridge, quay or pin it names — a notice post among the pin kinds, §2.7; tables/dossier/landmarks); nothing is created for a string alone. | tables/dossier/quarters, landmarks; §4.5 S6 typing | DR5; §10.4; V01; V08 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0584 | number | §7.7 item 16 | determinism | Quarter naming-claim floor = 0.5 of a perfect match (own value, calibratable). |  | DETERMINISM-0583 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0585 | rule | §7.7 whole-neighbourhood assignment | determinism | A whole-neighbourhood assignment sets the district's intent (layer 2) and its class mix — overriding the class-mix table's weights inside the district (§4.7 S10b, item 71) — while every building stays individually diversified by the class rules within the drift bounds (read against §4.7 S11's designation template, item 19, passed to BuildingGenerator.compose); a district-defining dossier institution is such an assignment with existence dossier, one district per instance (item 72, §4.5); assignments are events (persist, undo, migrate) and take precedence over class rules (O10 e); stable addresses for districts, neighbourhoods, plots and buildings are §6.3's. | §4.7 S10b, S11; BuildingGenerator.compose | §5.3 character row; V01; V02 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0586 | table | §7.7 Forms catalogue | determinism | Forms catalogue (should-have; the schema is the must-have hook): a form is a row { id, constraints, growthProgramme, signature } — constraints (site request, wall rings, plaza position, planned flag, gate count band), a growth programme (stage regimes and branch tables, §4.4), a morphometric signature (radial share of arterials, orthogonality index, street-density profile, block-area CV, enclosed share); initial rows: organic, bastide-grid, linear-street (growth anisotropy a = 8 along the main road, §4.4), radial, hill-acropolis, bridge-town, port-quay-parallel, monastic-close, island-city (shore destinations, §4.2 S3), confluence, twin-town, polycentric (two or three nuclei, §4.2 S3), citadel-and-port (item 101: a walled upper town on high ground over a walled lower port — two nuclei of §4.2 S3, the upper on a hill site primitive with its own enceinte, the lower on the quay front with its own, one arterial joining them); forms compose by growth stage (an organic core with a planned extension); O10 (g)'s classifier runs on the signature vector. | §4.4; §4.2 S3 | Intent.form; T1 intend site cards; O10 (g) classifier | DEFERRAL-CANDIDATE: "should-have" status wording |
| DETERMINISM-0587 | mechanism | §7.8 control registry | determinism | The control registry declares for every control its output metric (density → buildings per ha; wealthLevel → median plot area and greenery share; greenery → private green share), range, consuming stage (the earliest stage reading it), locality class (local — consumed at S10 or later, or spatially scoped: Adjust is local — or re-derive from Sk — the action shows the fate report first), continuity class (continuous or discrete, with a discrete control's levels) and a documentation anchor linked from the control as context help; the same registry fields (label, range, unit, output metric, consuming stage, locality class, continuity class, anchor) generate @plat/docs' reference pages. |  | Adjust locality (DETERMINISM-0316); @plat/docs; V09 panels |  |
| DETERMINISM-0588 | rule | §7.8 Layer controls (II.6, II.7; items 13-15) | determinism / V04, V05 | Islands: at TOWN and every village rung the control is disabled with the reason "islands only at CITY and METROPOLIS (II.6)"; at CITY and METROPOLIS it is the model feature recipe.layers.islands (off / on; consuming stage S7b; locality re-derive from S7b; discrete), the sky layer's visibility a separate view control (LayerSpec.visible, §8.4; no model effect) that shows the islands a settlement has and never creates one. Sewers: one control with two meanings by rung — at TOWN a model feature (auto / on / off; consuming stage S0; locality re-derive from the network stage, confined to the underground arena; discrete), at CITY and METROPOLIS a view control (the underground layer's LayerSpec.visible; no model effect) — hidden by default at every rung (II.7.1); both rows carry locality class and anchor in the registry and sit in the advanced panel outside the basic panel's count; elaborate (item 28) is no control: per-viewer view state (§2.9) deciding when a pure rung is derived, never what. | II.6, II.7.1; §8.4 LayerSpec | V04; V05; V09 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0589 | bar | §7.8 Monotone and continuous | determinism | CI sweep: each continuous control over 21 evenly spaced steps × 50 seeds at TOWN and CITY (METROPOLIS nightly): monotone — the mean of the control's metric moves in the declared direction between adjacent steps, a reversal of at most 2 standard errors allowed; continuous — no adjacent step moves the mean by more than ε = max(3 standard errors, 3 × the median adjacent change of the mean); per seed — at most 10 % of seeds jump by more than 5 × the median per-seed step at any one step; the formula is fixed now, the standard errors and medians are what the M1 sweep measures; continuous controls are smooth maps with no thresholds inside (VG's "1 vs 0.999" is the failure); discrete controls (walls on/off, form) declare their effect in the report. | DETERMINISM-0587 | §13 CI; §16 M1 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0590 | rule | §7.8 sweep fallback (J-DET-27) | determinism | A control failing its sweep: the failing interval = the smallest run of adjacent steps whose checks fail, widened by one step each side; (1) the control's expansion row (§7.7) is replaced over that interval by a smoothstep between the row's values at the interval's ends — a table change with provenance "sweep fallback" and a declared shift (§6.4) — and the sweep re-runs; (2) if still failing, it is declared discrete: the registry's continuity class becomes discrete with the levels at which the metric's mean is stable (steps between failing intervals, each represented by its median step), the basic and advanced panels show it stepped, and the report and release notes list it; the population control is continuous by construction (§10.2's prefix-stable roster) and checked the same way; its work-limit side is §5.1 (5)'s flat F-WORK rate. | DETERMINISM-0589; expansion rows | V09 panels |  |
| DETERMINISM-0591 | mechanism | §7.8 Proxy thumbnails (G-R4; O16 e) | determinism | "Surprise me" and the size / water controls preview six candidate towns as the towns' own S0-S4 runs (site, water, backbone at city LOD from those stages' display list in §8.5's distinct preview style) under the same deterministic work limits as a full generation (§5.1), so a proxy is bit-identical to the first stages of the town it previews; choosing one continues that recipe from its committed S4 state and asserts the S4 digest equals the proxy's; budgets on reference hardware (§12): all six within 1 s at TOWN (O16 (e)); at CITY and METROPOLIS progressively — the first within 300 ms, each within 250 ms at CITY and 500 ms at METROPOLIS. | §5.1 work limits; §8.5 preview style | V09 application; §12 budgets | TYPO: "a proxy is  bit-identical" double space |
| DETERMINISM-0592 | number | §7.8 | determinism / rendering §12 | Proxy budgets: six proxies within 1 s at TOWN; first within 300 ms, each within 250 ms at CITY and 500 ms at METROPOLIS. | §12 | DETERMINISM-0591 | possible restatement in §12 |
| DETERMINISM-0593 | rule | §7.8 control surface budget (R15) | determinism | The front door is one action; the basic panel holds ≤ 12 controls — seed (with the "Surprise me" dice as its button), size rung, water setting and side, walls, castle, gates, features (one checkbox strip — plaza, temple, shanty town, farms, greens: MFCG's first-level toggles, counted as one), planned ↔ organic, wealth level, greenery, style preset, labels; a feature left at auto is drawn as §4.2 S0 states and reported (§5.4); everything else behind an advanced panel; 100 % of parameters documented (registry test); new templates and presets are data, not code; release notes name known defects; the toolkit's T1 intend is this panel, not a thirteenth control: its site cards are the water setting and side (each card carrying a form of the forms catalogue and an era-and-region preset), its size the size rung, its two sliders the wealth level and the walls (item 101 (g)), its style the style preset — the toolkit adds no basic control, every slider keeps its sweep (J-A101.77). | DETERMINISM-0587; T1 | V09 application basic panel |  |
| DETERMINISM-0594 | number | §7.8 | determinism | Basic panel ≤ 12 controls. |  | DETERMINISM-0593 |  |
| DETERMINISM-0595 | rule | §7.8 Light controls (item 68; J-A68.71) | determinism / rendering §8.4 | The day and night switch (light), the sun's mode and height (sunMode, sunHeight) and the underground hand lantern (handLantern) are per-viewer view keys (§2.9) set from the view bar beside the layer toggles: view controls with no model effect, never logged, in no digest, outside the basic panel's count; each has its registry row (label, range, anchor, continuity class — sunHeight continuous, the others discrete), default §8.4's; the pointer moving the cursor sun is a transient input, never a key (§6.2). | §2.9 view keys; §8.4 | V09 view bar |  |
